1. How the system works

Four ideas explain most of what you will see. Read these boxes once before you start.

1.1 One set of books

Purpose: Explains why every report agrees with every other report.

Who can use it: Everyone.

Procedure:

  1. Record a purchase, sale, return, payment, receipt or expense in the normal screen.
  2. The moment it is posted (see the next box), the system writes a double-entry journal entry for it.
  3. Reports such as the Balance Sheet, Profit & Loss, Trial Balance and Cash Flow are added up from those journal entries.

How to verify: Open Reports → Balance Sheet; it should say the books are balanced.

Important noteIf a document was never posted, or was reversed, it will not appear in the reports. This is the most common reason numbers look wrong.

1.2 Draft, Posted and Reversed

Purpose: Explains the three states a purchase or sale can be in, and what each one does to stock and accounts.

Who can use it: Everyone can read this. Managers create and post; only admins reverse, correct or delete drafts.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases and Transactions → Sales (the Status column).

Procedure:

  1. Draft — you pressed Save Purchase or Save Sale. It has no invoice number and no effect on stock, ledgers or accounts. You can still edit it, post it, or (admin) delete it.
  2. Posted — you pressed the green Post button. The invoice number is assigned, stock moves, the customer or supplier ledger is updated and the journal entry is written. It can no longer be edited or deleted.
  3. Reversed — an admin reversed a posted document. The original stays on record marked reversed, and a cancelling journal entry is posted beside it.

How to verify: The Status column shows draft, or the payment status; a reversed document shows a reversed badge.

Important noteReturns, supplier payments, customer receipts, expenses and POS sales do not have a draft stage; they take effect when saved. Details are in each facility's box.

1.3 Stock cost: weighted average

Purpose: Explains how the system values stock and works out the cost of what you sell.

Who can use it: Everyone.

Procedure:

  1. Buy 10 units at 100, then 10 units at 140.
  2. You now hold 20 units worth 2,400, so each unit is costed at 120.
  3. Sell one unit: its cost is 120. This is an example only.

How to verify: Open Reports → Stock Valuation and compare the Avg Cost column with your purchases.

Important noteCosting is weighted average, not FIFO or LIFO. Batch-tracked items are also costed per batch where a batch cost exists.

1.4 Roles: Staff, Manager and Admin

Purpose: Shows what each of the three roles may do.

Who can use it: Everyone. An admin gives each person a role under Admin → Users.

Where to find it: Every menu item you may not use is shown greyed out; hover over it to see which role is needed.

Procedure:

  1. Staff can view Suppliers, Customers, Items, Purchases, Sales and the return pages; record supplier payments, customer receipts, bulk payments and bulk receipts; record purchase and sale returns; view delivery challans; and (HR) view employees and attendance and raise leave requests.
  2. Manager can also add and edit suppliers, customers, items, purchases and sales; post drafts; use POS and held bills; stock adjustments, transfers, reconciliations (create, count, finalize, reopen, cancel), labels and bulk import; purchase orders, quotations, expenses, journal entries, fixed assets, accounts, all reports, the Dashboard, and HR, attendance and payroll (create and edit).
  3. Admin can also reverse, correct and delete documents; approve and post reconciliations; reverse transfers; close fiscal years and open or close periods; manage users, warehouses, location access, financial accounts, business configuration, tax codes, the chart of accounts, the audit log, backup and restore, and post payroll.

How to verify: Sign in as the person and look at the menu; greyed-out items are the ones the role cannot use.

Important noteSome pages let a staff user open them but refuse the Save button; the system then shows a message such as 'Access denied. Only managers and admins can add sales.'

2. Signing in and your account

Signing in and out, registering, verifying your email, resetting and changing your password, and your profile.

2.1 Sign In

Purpose: Lets you into the system.

Who can use it: Anyone with a verified account.

Where to find it: The sign-in page; the Sign In link at the top right when you are signed out.

Procedure:

  1. Open the system address in your browser.
  2. Type your Email address.
  3. Type your Password.
  4. Press Sign In.
  5. You land on the Home page.

How to verify: You see the menu on the left and your name at the top right.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Invalid email or password!Check spelling and Caps Lock. The system does not say which of the two was wrong.
Please verify your email before signing in.Open the verification email, or use Resend Verification Email.
Too many login attempts.Wait a few minutes. Five attempts are allowed in five minutes from one computer.
Important noteDo not share your password. Every change you make is recorded under your name in the audit log.

2.2 Sign Out

Purpose: Ends your session so nobody else can use your account on this computer.

Who can use it: Any signed-in user.

Where to find it: Your name at the top right → Sign Out.

Procedure:

  1. Click your name at the top right.
  2. Choose Sign Out.

How to verify: You are taken to the sign-in page with the message 'Signed out successfully!'.

2.3 Sign Up (only when switched on)

Purpose: Lets a new person register themselves. It is normally switched off; an admin creates accounts instead.

Who can use it: Anyone, but only while the installation has self-registration enabled (setting ALLOW_SIGNUP).

Where to find it: The Sign Up page.

Procedure:

  1. Enter Name, Email and Password (at least 6 characters).
  2. Press Sign Up.
  3. Open the verification email and click its link.
  4. Sign in. A new account has the Staff role until an admin changes it.

How to verify: The message says a verification email was sent; after clicking the link you can sign in.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Registration is disabled. Contact the administrator.Self-registration is off. Ask an admin to create your account.
Email ... is already registered!Use Forgot Password instead.
Password must be at least 6 characters!Choose a longer password.

2.4 Verify Email

Purpose: Confirms that the email address is yours, so the account can be used.

Who can use it: The new user who received the email. An admin can also mark a user as verified.

Where to find it: The link inside the verification email.

Procedure:

  1. Open the email whose subject asks you to verify.
  2. Click the link.
  3. Wait for the confirmation message.
  4. Sign in.

How to verify: The message says the email was verified successfully and you can sign in.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Invalid or expired verification link!The link works for one hour. Use Resend Verification Email.
Email ... is already verified!Nothing to do; just sign in.

2.5 Resend Verification Email

Purpose: Sends a fresh verification link when the first one expired or never arrived.

Who can use it: Anyone with an unverified account.

Where to find it: The Resend Verification Email page.

Procedure:

  1. Type your Email.
  2. Press Resend Verification Email.
  3. Open the new email and click the link.

How to verify: The message says a new verification email has been sent.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Email ... not found!Check the spelling or ask an admin.
Email ... is already verified!Just sign in.

2.6 Forgot Password

Purpose: Emails you a link to choose a new password.

Who can use it: Anyone who knows their account email.

Where to find it: The Forgot Password page (linked from the sign-in page).

Procedure:

  1. Open Forgot Password.
  2. Type your Email.
  3. Press Send Reset Link.
  4. Open the email and click the link (see Reset Password).

How to verify: A message says that if the email is registered, a reset link has been sent. Check the spam folder too.

Important noteThe message is the same whether or not the email exists, so nobody can use it to find out who has an account. The link is valid for one hour. Five requests are allowed in five minutes.

2.7 Reset Password (from the email link)

Purpose: Sets a new password after Forgot Password.

Who can use it: The person who received the reset email.

Where to find it: The link in the reset email opens the Reset Password page.

Procedure:

  1. Type the New Password (at least 6 characters).
  2. Type it again in Confirm New Password.
  3. Press Reset Password.
  4. Sign in with the new password.

How to verify: The message says the password was reset successfully and asks you to sign in.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Invalid or expired reset link!Request a new link with Forgot Password.
Passwords do not match!Type both boxes again carefully.

2.8 My Profile

Purpose: Shows your name, email and whether the account is verified.

Who can use it: Any signed-in user.

Where to find it: Your name at the top right → My Profile.

Procedure:

  1. Click your name at the top right.
  2. Choose My Profile.
  3. Read the Account Details card.

How to verify: Name, Email and a Verified or Unverified badge are shown.

Important noteTo change your name or email, ask an admin (Admin → Users → Edit).

2.9 Change Password

Status: Known problem (found by reading the code, not by trying it in the browser): the Change Password form sends the fields current_password, new_password and confirm_password, but the page that receives them looks for different field names, so it is expected to answer 'Name cannot be empty!' and not change the password. Until this is fixed, change a password with Forgot Password, or ask an admin to set a new one under Admin → Users → Edit.

Purpose: Lets you replace your own password while signed in.

Who can use it: Any signed-in user. It is switched off in demo installations.

Where to find it: My Profile → Change Password card.

Procedure:

  1. Type your Current Password.
  2. Type the New Password (at least 6 characters).
  3. Type it again in Confirm New Password.
  4. Press Change Password.

How to verify: If it works on your installation, the page shows a success message; confirm by signing out and signing in with the new password.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Name cannot be empty!This is the expected answer from the known problem above; use Forgot Password instead.
Password must be at least 6 characters!Use a longer password.

3. Finding your way around

The home page, dashboard, search, notifications, printing, exporting, and the Manual and About pages.

3.1 Home page

Purpose: The first page after sign-in: a short overview with live figures and recent activity.

Who can use it: Everyone. Some links inside it work only for managers.

Where to find it: The TradeFlow name at the top left, or sign in.

Procedure:

  1. Sign in, or click the brand name at the top left.
  2. Read the overview: today's sales and purchases, receivables, payables, items below reorder level, batches nearing expiry.
  3. Read the recent activity list (latest sale or purchase, payment received, stock transfer, and low-stock or expiry warnings).

How to verify: The receivable and payable figures agree with the customer and supplier ledgers.

3.2 Global Search

Purpose: Finds a supplier, customer or item from any page.

Who can use it: Any signed-in user.

Where to find it: The search box at the top of every page: 'Search supplier, customer, item...'.

Procedure:

  1. Click the search box.
  2. Type part of a name, contact or address.
  3. Pick a result from the list that drops down; it opens that record's page.

How to verify: The page for the chosen supplier, customer or item opens.

3.3 Notifications: view

Purpose: Shows alerts addressed to you: low stock, leave requests and decisions, payroll finalized or cancelled.

Who can use it: Any signed-in user sees their own notifications. Low-stock alerts go to managers and admins; payroll alerts to admins.

Where to find it: The bell icon at the top right → Notifications.

Procedure:

  1. Click the bell icon.
  2. Read the When, Severity, Title and Message columns.

How to verify: The red count on the bell falls as you read items.

3.4 Notifications: mark read

Purpose: Clears an alert after you have dealt with it.

Who can use it: Any signed-in user, for their own notifications.

Where to find it: Notifications page.

Procedure:

  1. Press Mark read beside one notification, or Mark all read at the top to clear all of them.

How to verify: The notification is no longer counted on the bell.

Important noteA new low-stock alert for the same item is not repeated while an earlier one is still unread.

3.5 Print or Save as PDF

Purpose: Prints a table or report, or saves it as a PDF file, using your browser.

Who can use it: Anyone who can open the page.

Where to find it: A Print PDF or Print button at the top of most lists and reports.

Procedure:

  1. Set the filters so the page shows what you want.
  2. Press Print PDF (or Print).
  3. In the browser's print window, choose a printer, or choose Save as PDF as the destination.
  4. Press Print or Save.

How to verify: The preview shows a titled table with the company name and no action buttons.

Important noteThe system does not create PDF files itself; the browser does. Sale and purchase invoices open as printable pages; POS receipts print on 80 mm paper (see POS: print a receipt).

3.6 Export to CSV or Excel

Purpose: Downloads a list or report as a spreadsheet file.

Who can use it: Managers and admins for every export except the Item Ledger, which any verified user may export.

Where to find it: CSV and Excel buttons on Suppliers, Customers, Item Ledger, Supplier Ledger, Customer Ledger, Stock Valuation and the Reports page.

Procedure:

  1. Apply any date range or search you want.
  2. Press CSV or Excel.
  3. Open the downloaded file in a spreadsheet program.

How to verify: The file contains the same rows and totals as the screen.

3.7 Dashboard

Purpose: Shows business totals and charts: payable, receivable, sales and profit, top items, top suppliers and customers, payment methods, expenses, and stock alerts.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Dashboard in the menu.

Procedure:

  1. Open Dashboard.
  2. Use the category filter if you only want one product category.
  3. Use the buttons at the top: View Reports, Expired Items, Near to Expiry, Low Stock Items, Business Insights.
  4. Use Record Payment and Record Receipt under the Payable and Receivable cards to go straight to those screens.

How to verify: The Payable and Receivable cards agree with the customer and supplier ledgers.

Important noteFigures come from posted documents only; drafts are not counted.

3.8 Business Insights

Purpose: Lists customers who owe you the most and for how long, suppliers you owe, and items that are low in stock.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Dashboard → Business Insights.

Procedure:

  1. Open the Dashboard.
  2. Press Business Insights.
  3. Read the Customer (Balance Due, Oldest Sale Age), Supplier and Item (Current Stock) tables.
  4. Press Back to Dashboard when done.

How to verify: Balances agree with the customer and supplier ledgers.

3.9 Create Purchase List

Purpose: Prints a shopping list of items that are below their reorder level.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Dashboard → Low Stock Items table → Create Purchase List.

Procedure:

  1. On the Dashboard, scroll to Low Stock Items.
  2. Press Create Purchase List.
  3. Review the list, then press Print PDF.

How to verify: Each listed item has Current Stock at or below Reorder Level.

3.10 Open the User Manual and the About page

Purpose: Reads this manual, or a short description of what the system does.

Who can use it: Anyone, even without signing in.

Where to find it: Manual and About in the menu.

Procedure:

  1. Click Manual (or About) in the menu.
  2. In the Manual use the Contents list or sidebar to jump to a module or a facility.
  3. Press Print at the top to print the manual in the language you are reading.

How to verify: The page opens with the English text and a language switch.

3.11 Switch the Manual or About page between English and Roman Urdu

Purpose: Shows the same pages in English or in Urdu written in English letters.

Who can use it: Anyone.

Where to find it: The English and اردو (Roman) buttons at the top of the Manual and About pages.

Procedure:

  1. Press اردو (Roman) to read in Roman Urdu, or English to go back.

How to verify: The text changes language at once.

Important noteEvery visit starts in English; your choice is not remembered.

3.12 Search, sort and page through long lists

Purpose: Finds records quickly on pages that list suppliers, customers, items, purchases, sales and similar records.

Who can use it: Anyone who can open the page.

Where to find it: The search box at the top of a list, the column headings, and the page links under the table.

Procedure:

  1. Type part of a name in the list's search box and press Search; press Clear (where shown) to see everything again.
  2. On Suppliers, Customers and Items click a column heading (for example Name or Opening) to sort; click it again to reverse the order.
  3. Use the page links under the table to move to the next page; lists show ten rows per page.

How to verify: The table shows only matching rows, in the order you chose.

Important noteThe total row on the Suppliers and Customers pages covers the rows matching the current search, not just the visible page.

4. Users and permissions

Adding and managing the people who sign in. Admin menu: Users. Only admins can open these pages.

4.1 View Users

Purpose: Lists every user with role and verified status.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Users

Procedure:

  1. Open Admin → Users.
  2. Read the #, Name, Email, Role, Status and Actions columns.
  3. Press Print PDF to print the list.

How to verify: Your own row shows a You badge.

4.2 Create User

Purpose: Adds a new person who can sign in.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Users → Create User

Procedure:

  1. Press Create User.
  2. Enter Full Name and Email Address.
  3. Enter a Password (at least 6 characters) and retype it in Confirm Password.
  4. Choose the Role: Staff, Manager or Admin.
  5. Tick Mark as Verified if the person should be able to sign in immediately.
  6. Press Create User.

How to verify: The message says the user was created successfully and the user appears in the list.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Name, email, and password are required.Fill all three.
Passwords do not match.Retype both boxes.
Email ... is already registered.Use a different email or edit the existing user.
Password must be at least 6 characters.Use a longer password.
Important noteThe page also shows a Role Permissions table. Choose the lowest role that lets the person do their job.

4.3 Edit User

Purpose: Changes a user's name, email, password, role or verified flag.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Users → the pencil button on a row

Procedure:

  1. Press the pencil (Edit user) button.
  2. Change Full Name, Email Address or Role as needed.
  3. Type a New Password only if you want to replace it; leave it blank to keep the current one.
  4. Tick or untick Verified.
  5. Press Save Changes.

How to verify: The message says the user was updated successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Name and email are required.Fill both.
Email ... is already in use.Choose another email.
Password must be at least 6 characters.Use a longer password.

4.4 Change a User's Role

Purpose: Moves a person between Staff, Manager and Admin quickly.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Users → the role dropdown on a row

Procedure:

  1. Find the person's row.
  2. Pick the new role in the dropdown.
  3. Press the green tick (Save role).

How to verify: The message reads '<name>'s role changed from ... to ...'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
You cannot change your own role.Ask another admin.
Invalid role.Choose Staff, Manager or Admin.

4.5 Mark a User Verified or Unverified

Purpose: Lets you allow or block sign-in without deleting the account.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Users → the verify button on a row

Procedure:

  1. Press the button in the row: it reads Mark verified for an unverified user and Mark unverified for a verified one.

How to verify: The Status column changes and the message says the user is now verified or unverified.

Important noteAn unverified user is told to verify their email and cannot use the system.

4.6 Delete User

Purpose: Removes a user account.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Users → the trash button on a row

Procedure:

  1. Press the trash button.
  2. Confirm the question.

How to verify: The message says the user was deleted and the row disappears.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
You cannot delete your own account.Ask another admin.
Important noteIf you only want to stop someone signing in, mark them unverified instead; it can be undone.

5. Warehouses and location access

Where stock is kept and which warehouses each user may use. Admin menu.

5.1 Add Warehouse

Purpose: Creates another stock location. Purchases, sales, transfers, adjustments and counts all name a warehouse.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Warehouses → Add a warehouse

Procedure:

  1. Open Admin → Warehouses.
  2. Type the Name (illustrative example: 'Karachi Store').
  3. Optionally type the Address.
  4. Press Add Warehouse.

How to verify: The new warehouse appears in the table and in the Warehouse drop-downs.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Warehouse name is required.Type a name.
A warehouse named ... already exists.Use a different name.
Important noteWith only one warehouse, the Warehouse Transfers menu is hidden. It appears once a second warehouse exists.

5.2 Deactivate or Reactivate a Warehouse

Purpose: Stops a warehouse being offered for new documents, or brings it back.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Warehouses → Deactivate / Reactivate in the Action column

Procedure:

  1. Find the warehouse.
  2. Press Deactivate (or Reactivate for an inactive one).

How to verify: The Status column changes and the message says the warehouse is now active or inactive.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... is the default warehouse and cannot be deactivated.The Default warehouse (marked with a Default badge) is locked.

5.3 Grant a User Access to a Warehouse

Purpose: Limits a manager or staff user to particular warehouses.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Location Access

Procedure:

  1. Open Admin → Location Access.
  2. Find the user's row.
  3. Pick a warehouse in Grant a warehouse.
  4. Press Grant. Repeat for each warehouse the user may use.

How to verify: The message reads '<user> may now access <warehouse>' and the row lists it.

Important noteA user with no grants can use every warehouse. The first grant switches that user to only the granted ones. Admins are never restricted and do not appear on this page.

5.4 Revoke a User's Warehouse Access

Purpose: Removes one granted warehouse from a user.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Location Access

Procedure:

  1. Find the user's row.
  2. Press the small round x (Revoke) button on the warehouse you want to take away, and confirm.

How to verify: The message says 'Access revoked.'

Important noteRevoking the last grant makes the user unrestricted again, which means access to every warehouse.

6. Cash and bank accounts, and business configuration

Where money is held, and the product categories and fields your business uses. Admin menu.

6.1 Add a Cash or Bank Account

Purpose: Creates an account that payments, receipts, expenses and POS sales can be posted to.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Financial Accounts → Add Account

Procedure:

  1. Press Add Account.
  2. Choose Account Type: Control (a heading, never posted to), Subsidiary (sits under a control account) or Standalone.
  3. Type the Account Name (an example: 'UBL').
  4. Choose the Account Subtype (Cash or Bank) where the page offers it.
  5. For a Subsidiary choose the Parent Control Account.
  6. Optionally enter an Opening Balance.
  7. Press Create Account.

How to verify: The account appears under its control heading or under Standalone Accounts, and in the Account drop-down on payment, receipt and expense screens.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Account name is required!Type a name.
An account named ... already exists!Names must be unique, ignoring capitals and spaces.
A subsidiary account must belong to a control account!Choose the Parent Control Account.
Invalid opening balance!Enter a number.
Important noteAn opening balance is a real accounting entry. Enter it before trading starts.

6.2 Edit or Reactivate a Cash or Bank Account

Purpose: Renames an account, moves it, changes its opening balance, or brings an inactive account back.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Financial Accounts → Edit (pencil) or Edit / Reactivate under Inactive Accounts

Procedure:

  1. Press Edit beside the account.
  2. Change the fields.
  3. Press the save button.

How to verify: The message says the account was updated successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
An account cannot be its own parent!Pick a different parent control account.
An account named ... already exists!Use a unique name.

6.3 Delete a Cash or Bank Account

Purpose: Removes an account that was created by mistake.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Financial Accounts → the trash button

Procedure:

  1. Press the trash button beside the account.
  2. Confirm 'Delete ...?'.

How to verify: The message says the account was deleted successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete account ... it has subsidiary accounts!Delete or move the subsidiaries first.
Cannot delete account ... it has transactions!Edit it and make it inactive instead.

6.4 Business Categories: view, enable and disable

Purpose: Lists the product categories your business uses and lets you switch each on or off. An item must belong to an enabled business category.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Business Configuration

Procedure:

  1. Open Admin → Business Configuration.
  2. Use the Enabled / Disabled switch on a category card.

How to verify: Only enabled categories appear in the Category drop-down on the Items page.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
No business categories are enabled. Please enable categories in Admin Settings before adding items!Enable at least one category here.

6.5 Add a Business Category

Purpose: Creates a new product category.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Business Configuration → Add Category

Procedure:

  1. Press Add Category.
  2. Type the Category Name (an example: 'Clothing').
  3. Optionally type a Description.
  4. Press Create Category.

How to verify: The new card appears in the list.

6.6 Edit a Business Category

Purpose: Changes a category's name or description.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Business Configuration → Edit on the category card

Procedure:

  1. Press Edit.
  2. Change the name or description.
  3. Press Update Category.

How to verify: The card shows the new name.

6.7 Switch Batch Tracking and Expiry on a Category

Purpose: Decides whether items in this category can be tracked batch by batch and carry expiry dates (for example medicines).

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Business Configuration → Fields on the category card → Stock tracking

Procedure:

  1. Press Fields on the category.
  2. In Stock tracking, tick Batch tracking and/or Stock expiry.
  3. The change is saved straight away.

How to verify: When this category is chosen on the Add Item form, the Batch tracked option applies to it.

Important noteAn existing item only becomes batch-tracked when you press Enable Batch Tracking on its Edit Item page.

6.8 Add a Product Field to a Category

Purpose: Adds an extra box to the item form for that category, such as Size or Colour.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Business Configuration → Fields → Add Field

Procedure:

  1. Press Fields on the category, then Add Field.
  2. Type the Field Label (what people see) and a Field Name (code).
  3. Choose the Field Type: Text, Number, Date, Dropdown, Yes/No or Textarea.
  4. Optionally set Tab Name, Required field? and Searchable?.
  5. Press the save button.

How to verify: The field appears in the table and on the item form for that category.

6.9 Delete a Product Field

Purpose: Removes an extra field from a category.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Business Configuration → Fields → Delete

Procedure:

  1. Open the category's Fields page.
  2. Press Delete beside the field.
  3. Confirm.

How to verify: The field disappears from the table.

7. Audit, backup, reset and developer tools

The audit log, saving and restoring data, starting fresh, and the technical Developer Tasks panel.

7.1 Audit Log

Purpose: A record of who did what and when.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Audit Log

Procedure:

  1. Open Admin → Audit Log.
  2. Optionally pick an action in Filter: to show only that kind of entry.
  3. Read When, User, Action, Entity and Details.
  4. Use Print PDF to print the page.

How to verify: The newest entries are at the top.

Important noteThe page has no edit or delete buttons. It does not offer an export; use Print PDF.

7.2 Download a Backup

Purpose: Saves every table into one JSON file.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Backup & Restore → Backup Database

Procedure:

  1. Open Admin → Backup & Restore.
  2. Press Download Backup (.json).
  3. Save the file somewhere other than this computer's only disk.

How to verify: A .json file is downloaded. The page also lists each table and its row count.

Important noteTake a backup every week and before anything that cannot be undone.

7.3 Restore from a Backup

Purpose: Replaces everything in the system with the contents of a backup file.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Backup & Restore → Restore Database

Procedure:

  1. Download a fresh backup first.
  2. Press the file box under Select backup file and choose the .json backup.
  3. Press Restore Now (confirm if the browser asks).

How to verify: The message says the database was restored successfully and gives a row count.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
No file selected.Choose a file.
Invalid file — not a valid JSON backup.Use a file downloaded from Download Backup.
Restore failed (no changes were applied).Nothing was changed; check the file and try again.
Important noteRestore replaces the current data. Anything entered since the backup was taken is lost.

7.4 Delete All Transactions

Purpose: Clears the trading history but keeps the people and products you trade with, for example when a trial period ends and real trading begins.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Backup & Restore → Delete Transactions

Procedure:

  1. Download a backup first.
  2. In the confirmation box type exactly DELETE ALL TRANSACTIONS.
  3. Press Delete Transactions.

How to verify: The message says all transactions were deleted and tells you how many suppliers, customers and items were kept.

Important noteKept: users, suppliers, customers, items, categories, expense categories, the chart of accounts, tax codes, fiscal years and periods, and cash/bank accounts. Cleared: invoices, payments, journals, assets and other records, and the opening balances and stock of suppliers, customers and items are set to zero. Invoice numbering restarts. A closed fiscal year is reopened.
Important noteThis cannot be undone.

7.5 Delete Everything

Purpose: Empties the system of all business data so it can be handed to someone who will enter their own.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Admin → Backup & Restore → Delete Everything

Procedure:

  1. Download a backup first.
  2. In the confirmation box type exactly DELETE ALL DATA.
  3. Press Delete Everything.

How to verify: The message says all business data was deleted and the system is empty and ready to be set up.

Important noteOnly user accounts are kept. The standard chart of accounts, tax codes, this year's periods and the four cash/bank accounts are created again; everything else, including warehouses, business categories and HR records, must be set up again.
Important noteThis cannot be undone.

7.6 Developer Tasks panel

Purpose: A password-protected technical area for the person who maintains the installation: database tools, diagnostics, logs, environment settings and optional modules.

Who can use it: Technical staff only. It works only when the installation has the panel switched on (ENABLE_DEVELOPER_PANEL), is usually limited to the local machine, needs the developer password, and signs out after a period of inactivity.

Where to find it: Your name at the top right → Developer Tasks.

Procedure:

  1. Choose Developer Tasks from your name menu.
  2. Type the Developer Password and press Unlock Developer Panel.
  3. Pick a tool: Database Manager, Schema Sync, Data Display, Diagnostics, Statistics, Logs, Optional Modules or Environment.
  4. Press Logout when finished.

How to verify: After the correct password you see the Developer Tasks Panel.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Incorrect password.Retype it.
Too many failed attempts. Try again in N minutes.Wait; five wrong attempts lock the panel for 15 minutes.
The page is refused or not found.The panel is switched off or limited to the server computer.
Important noteDo not change environment or database settings here unless you know exactly what you are doing; they affect everyone.

7.7 Switch HR, Attendance, Leave and Payroll modules on or off

Purpose: Shows or hides the optional HR menus. All four are off by default.

Who can use it: Technical staff using the Developer Tasks panel.

Where to find it: Developer Tasks → Optional Modules

Procedure:

  1. Open Optional Modules.
  2. Turn HR Module on first; Attendance, Leave and Payroll all depend on it.
  3. Turn on the others you need.
  4. Reload the page; the HR & Payroll menu appears.

How to verify: The module's status reads ON and its menu items appear for users who have permission.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Turn HR Module on first — ... depends on it.Switch HR on before the others.
Important noteTurning a module off hides its menus and refuses its pages; no records are deleted, and payroll journal entries already posted stay in the books.

7.8 Developer Tasks: Environment settings

Purpose: Shows installation settings such as the application name, company name, time zone, currency, whether sign-up is allowed, the fiscal-year start month and the mail server, plus editable configuration values stored in the database.

Who can use it: Technical staff using the Developer Tasks panel.

Where to find it: Developer Tasks → Environment

Procedure:

  1. Open Environment in the Developer Tasks panel.
  2. Read the settings; the database address is hidden.
  3. To change an editable configuration value, edit it and save; read-only values cannot be changed.

How to verify: The message reads 'Configuration <name> updated to <value>', with a warning if an application restart is needed.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Configuration <name> is read-only.That value cannot be changed here.
Configuration <name> not found.Reload the page.
Important noteCompany name, currency, time zone and the fiscal-year start month are decided when the system is installed. Choose them before entering data: changing the fiscal-year start month later does not move fiscal years that already exist.

8. Suppliers and Customers

Each supplier and customer has a list page, a ledger, and its own payments or receipts (see Buying and Selling).

8.1 View and Search Suppliers

Purpose: Lists every supplier with opening balance, bills, paid and balance, with a total row.

Who can use it: Any verified user can view; only managers and admins can add or change.

Where to find it: Parties → Suppliers

Procedure:

  1. Open Parties → Suppliers.
  2. Type part of a name, contact or address in the search box and press Search.
  3. Click a column heading (ID, Name, Contact, Address, Opening) to sort.
  4. Use Print to print the list.

How to verify: The bottom row shows the totals for the rows currently listed.

Important noteBills is the total of this supplier's posted purchases. The balance is what you still owe.

8.2 Add a Supplier

Purpose: Creates a new supplier, optionally with the balance carried from before you started using the system.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Parties → Suppliers → Add New Supplier

Procedure:

  1. Fill Name, Contact and Address (all required).
  2. Enter an Opening Balance if there was one (what you owe them).
  3. Press Add Supplier.

How to verify: The message says the supplier was added successfully and the row appears in the list.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
All fields are required!Fill name, contact and address.
Enter a valid phone number (7-15 digits; + - ( ) and spaces are fine).Correct the contact.
Supplier already exists!Search for the existing record instead.
Opening balance must be a valid number!Type a number.
You do not have permission to add suppliers.Ask a manager or admin.
Important noteThe opening balance is posted to the books against Opening Balance Equity, so it appears on the Balance Sheet and in the supplier's ledger. Enter it before recording transactions.

8.3 Edit a Supplier

Purpose: Corrects a supplier's name, contact, address or opening balance.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Parties → Suppliers → the pencil button on the row

Procedure:

  1. Press the pencil (Edit supplier) button.
  2. Change the fields.
  3. Press Update Supplier (or Cancel to leave without saving).
  4. View Full Ledger opens the ledger.

How to verify: The message says the supplier was updated successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
All fields are required!Fill every field.
Opening balance must be a valid number!Type a number.
Important noteChanging the opening balance reverses the old opening entry and posts a new one, so it is not counted twice.

8.4 Delete a Supplier

Purpose: Removes a supplier that was entered by mistake and has no history.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Parties → Suppliers → the trash button on the row

Procedure:

  1. Press the trash button.
  2. Confirm 'Delete this supplier?'.

How to verify: The message says the supplier was deleted successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete supplier with associated purchases!The record has history; keep it.
Cannot delete supplier with associated payments!Delete or reverse those first, or keep the record.

8.5 Export the Supplier List

Purpose: Downloads the supplier list as CSV or Excel.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Parties → Suppliers → CSV / Excel buttons

Procedure:

  1. Open Parties → Suppliers.
  2. Press CSV or Excel.

How to verify: A file downloads with one row per supplier.

8.6 Supplier Ledger: view and filter

Purpose: Shows every entry for one supplier: date, type, description, debit, credit and running balance, with a Total row.

Who can use it: Any verified user can view; only managers and admins can add or change.

Where to find it: Parties → Suppliers → the book button (View ledger) on the row

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger from the list.
  2. Pick a Start Date and End Date and press Filter to see one period.
  3. Use Print PDF to print it.

How to verify: The last Balance equals the balance shown on the supplier list (for the full date range).

Important noteThe balance is what what you owe them.

8.7 Add a Manual Adjustment to a Supplier Ledger

Purpose: Adds a correcting line directly to this ledger when no document covers it.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Supplier Ledger → Manual Adjustment

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger.
  2. In Manual Adjustment choose the Date.
  3. Choose the Type: Credit (Bill) adds to what you owe; Debit (Payment) reduces it.
  4. Enter the Amount and an optional Description.
  5. Press Add.

How to verify: The message says 'Ledger adjustment added!' and a line of type Adjustment appears.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Valid date, type and amount are required for adjustment!Fill date, type and amount.
Access denied. Only managers and admins can add ledger adjustments.Ask a manager or admin.
Important noteA manual adjustment changes only this supplier's ledger; no journal entry is posted. Because the general ledger does not see it, the Subledger Reconciliation report may show a difference. Use it only as a last resort.

8.8 Delete a Manual Adjustment from a Supplier Ledger

Purpose: Removes an adjustment line added by mistake.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Supplier Ledger → the delete button on an Adjustment line

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger.
  2. Press the delete button on the Adjustment line and confirm.

How to verify: The message says 'Adjustment deleted!' and the balance is recalculated.

Important noteOnly Adjustment lines can be deleted this way. Purchases, sales, payments and receipts are never deleted from a ledger.

8.9 Export a Supplier Ledger

Purpose: Downloads one ledger as CSV or Excel.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Supplier Ledger → CSV / Excel

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger, set any date filter.
  2. Press CSV or Excel.

How to verify: The downloaded file matches the screen.

8.10 View and Search Customers

Purpose: Lists every customer with opening balance, sales, received and balance, with a total row.

Who can use it: Any verified user can view; only managers and admins can add or change.

Where to find it: Parties → Customers

Procedure:

  1. Open Parties → Customers.
  2. Type part of a name, contact or address in the search box and press Search.
  3. Click a column heading (ID, Name, Contact, Address, Opening) to sort.
  4. Use Print to print the list.

How to verify: The bottom row shows the totals for the rows currently listed.

Important noteSales is net of sale returns (sales minus returns against active sales); it is not the gross sales figure. The balance is what the customer still owes.

8.11 Add a Customer

Purpose: Creates a new customer, optionally with the balance carried from before you started using the system.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Parties → Customers → Add New Customer

Procedure:

  1. Fill Name, Contact and Address (all required).
  2. Enter an Opening Balance if there was one (what they owe you).
  3. Press Add Customer.

How to verify: The message says the customer was added successfully and the row appears in the list.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
All fields are required!Fill name, contact and address.
Enter a valid phone number (7-15 digits; + - ( ) and spaces are fine).Correct the contact.
Customer already exists!Search for the existing record instead.
Opening balance must be a valid number!Type a number.
You do not have permission to add customers.Ask a manager or admin.
Important noteThe opening balance is posted to the books against Opening Balance Equity, so it appears on the Balance Sheet and in the customer's ledger. Enter it before recording transactions.

8.12 Edit a Customer

Purpose: Corrects a customer's name, contact, address or opening balance.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Parties → Customers → the pencil button on the row

Procedure:

  1. Press the pencil (Edit customer) button.
  2. Change the fields.
  3. Press Update Customer (or Cancel to leave without saving).
  4. View Full Ledger opens the ledger.

How to verify: The message says the customer was updated successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
All fields are required!Fill every field.
Opening balance must be a valid number!Type a number.
Important noteChanging the opening balance reverses the old opening entry and posts a new one, so it is not counted twice.

8.13 Delete a Customer

Purpose: Removes a customer that was entered by mistake and has no history.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Parties → Customers → the trash button on the row

Procedure:

  1. Press the trash button.
  2. Confirm 'Delete this customer?'.

How to verify: The message says the customer was deleted successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete customer with associated sales!The record has history; keep it.
Cannot delete customer with associated receipts!Delete or reverse those first, or keep the record.

8.14 Export the Customer List

Purpose: Downloads the customer list as CSV or Excel.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Parties → Customers → CSV / Excel buttons

Procedure:

  1. Open Parties → Customers.
  2. Press CSV or Excel.

How to verify: A file downloads with one row per customer.

8.15 Customer Ledger: view and filter

Purpose: Shows every entry for one customer: date, type, description, debit, credit and running balance, with a Total row.

Who can use it: Any verified user can view; only managers and admins can add or change.

Where to find it: Parties → Customers → the book button (View ledger) on the row

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger from the list.
  2. Pick a Start Date and End Date and press Filter to see one period.
  3. Use Print PDF to print it.

How to verify: The last Balance equals the balance shown on the customer list (for the full date range).

Important noteThe balance is what what they owe you.

8.16 Add a Manual Adjustment to a Customer Ledger

Purpose: Adds a correcting line directly to this ledger when no document covers it.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Customer Ledger → Manual Adjustment

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger.
  2. In Manual Adjustment choose the Date.
  3. Choose the Type: Debit (Sale) adds to what they owe; Credit (Receipt) reduces it.
  4. Enter the Amount and an optional Description.
  5. Press Add.

How to verify: The message says 'Ledger adjustment added!' and a line of type Adjustment appears.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Valid date, type and amount are required for adjustment!Fill date, type and amount.
Access denied. Only managers and admins can add ledger adjustments.Ask a manager or admin.
Important noteA manual adjustment changes only this customer's ledger; no journal entry is posted. Because the general ledger does not see it, the Subledger Reconciliation report may show a difference. Use it only as a last resort.

8.17 Delete a Manual Adjustment from a Customer Ledger

Purpose: Removes an adjustment line added by mistake.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Customer Ledger → the delete button on an Adjustment line

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger.
  2. Press the delete button on the Adjustment line and confirm.

How to verify: The message says 'Adjustment deleted!' and the balance is recalculated.

Important noteOnly Adjustment lines can be deleted this way. Purchases, sales, payments and receipts are never deleted from a ledger.

8.18 Export a Customer Ledger

Purpose: Downloads one ledger as CSV or Excel.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Customer Ledger → CSV / Excel

Procedure:

  1. Open the ledger, set any date filter.
  2. Press CSV or Excel.

How to verify: The downloaded file matches the screen.

9. Inventory: items, batches and labels

Creating and maintaining items, batch and expiry tracking, bulk import and labels. Menu: Inventory.

9.1 View and Search Items

Purpose: Lists every item with unit, barcode, SKU, opening stock, current stock, reorder level and prices.

Who can use it: Any verified user can view; only managers and admins can add or change.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items (the page is titled Inventory)

Procedure:

  1. Open Inventory → Items.
  2. Type in the search box and/or pick a business category, then press Search.
  3. Click a column heading to sort.
  4. Use Print PDF to print.

How to verify: Current Stock agrees with the Stock Valuation report.

9.2 Add an Item

Purpose: Creates a product or a service you sell or buy.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → Add New Item

Procedure:

  1. Type the Item Name and choose the Category (only enabled business categories are offered).
  2. Choose the Unit of Measure and the Item Type: Stock Item (stock is counted) or Service Item (no inventory).
  3. For a stock item enter Opening Stock (what is on the shelf today) and Reorder Level.
  4. Enter Purchase Price and Sale Price per unit. Opening stock needs a purchase price above zero.
  5. Optionally enter Barcode / QR Code and SKU, and any Category-Specific Fields shown.
  6. Press Add Item.

How to verify: The message says the item was added successfully; the item appears in the list with the opening stock as Current Stock.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Name and Category are required!Fill both.
Reorder Level is required for Stock items!Enter it.
Opening stock requires a purchase price greater than 0!Enter the cost per unit.
Barcode '...' is already used by another item.Barcodes and SKUs must be unique.
No business categories are enabled.An admin must enable one under Admin → Business Configuration.
Important noteOpening stock is placed in the default warehouse and valued at the purchase price you enter. Quantities are counted in the item's base unit.

9.3 Add a Batch-Tracked Item with Batch-wise Opening Stock

Purpose: For medicines and similar goods: records opening stock batch by batch with expiry dates.

Who can use it: Managers and admins. The category must have batch tracking switched on.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → Add New Item → Batch tracked

Procedure:

  1. Fill the item as in Add an Item.
  2. Tick Batch tracked (medicines / items with expiry).
  3. Press Add Batch for each batch and fill Batch No, Expiry Date, Qty and Purchase Rate. Leave Batch No blank for stock with no number (it becomes the Unknown batch).
  4. Press Add Item.

How to verify: Stock Valuation shows the item with a batches link listing each batch, warehouse, expiry and quantity.

Important noteThe item's opening stock is the total of the batch rows. When you sell, the system automatically uses the batch that expires first; expired batches are skipped by that automatic choice.

9.4 Add Alternate Units to an Item

Purpose: Lets one item be bought or sold in more than one unit, for example a box and a strip of the same medicine.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → Add New Item or Edit Item → Alternate Units

Procedure:

  1. In Alternate Units press Add Unit.
  2. Type the Unit Name (an example: Box).
  3. Type how many base units it equals in = Base Units (an example: 10).
  4. Optionally enter a Purchase Price and Sale Price for that unit.
  5. Press Add Item or Update Item.

How to verify: Purchase, Sale, Quotation and Purchase Order lines for that item show a Unit dropdown.

Important noteStock is always held in the base unit; a line in another unit is converted by its factor. Returns go back in the unit of the original line.

9.5 Edit an Item

Purpose: Corrects an item's name, category, prices, barcode, SKU, reorder level, unit or opening stock.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → the pencil button (Edit item)

Procedure:

  1. Press the pencil button on the item.
  2. Change the fields. Current Balance Stock is shown for information.
  3. Press Update Item.

How to verify: The message says the item was updated successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot edit opening stock after transactions have been recorded!Use a stock adjustment instead.
Barcode '...' is already used by another item.Use a unique code.
This item is batch-tracked: its stock is held batch by batch...Change batch quantities with a purchase or stock adjustment.

9.6 Enable Batch Tracking on an Existing Item

Purpose: Converts an ordinary item to batch tracking.

Who can use it: Admin only. The item's category must have batch tracking switched on.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → Edit Item → Batch/Lot Tracking

Procedure:

  1. Open the item with Edit item.
  2. In Batch/Lot Tracking press Enable Batch Tracking and confirm.

How to verify: The message says batch tracking was enabled for the item and what happened to its existing stock.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Batch tracking is not enabled for the '...' category.An admin must switch it on in Admin → Business Configuration → Fields first.
Important noteDo this before you start trading the item in batches; read the message that follows to see how existing stock was treated.

9.7 Delete an Item

Purpose: Removes an item that was created by mistake and has never been bought or sold.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → trash button

Procedure:

  1. Press the trash button on the item.
  2. Confirm 'Are you sure?'.

How to verify: The message says the item was deleted successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete item with associated purchases or sales!Keep the item; it has history.

9.8 Item Ledger

Purpose: Shows every movement of one item: date, type, reference, party, batch, rate, stock in, stock out, value and running balance, plus where the item is right now by warehouse.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → the book button (View ledger)

Procedure:

  1. Press View ledger on the item.
  2. Read Where This Item Is Right Now for stock per warehouse.
  3. Pick Start Date and End Date and press Filter; press Clear to reset.
  4. Use CSV, Excel or Print.

How to verify: The final Balance equals the item's Current Stock.

9.9 Send a Low Stock Alert Email

Purpose: Emails a list of items at or below their reorder level.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Items → Low Stock Alert

Procedure:

  1. Press Low Stock Alert at the top of the Items page.

How to verify: The message says 'Low stock alert sent for N item(s).'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Email not configured — cannot send alert.Ask whoever maintains the installation to set up the mail account.

9.10 Bulk Import Items, Customers or Suppliers

Purpose: Adds many records at once from a CSV, Excel or JSON file.

Who can use it: Managers and admins (the menu item is greyed out for staff).

Where to find it: Inventory → Bulk Import

Procedure:

  1. Open Inventory → Bulk Import.
  2. Under What do you want to import? choose Items / Products, Customers or Suppliers.
  3. Press Choose File and pick a .csv, .xlsx, .xls or .json file whose columns match the template examples on the page.
  4. Press Import Now.
  5. Read Recent Imports; press View errors on a row to see failed rows and the reason.

How to verify: Recent Imports shows the success and failure counts for your file.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
File is empty.Check the file has a header row and data.
No file selected.Choose a file first.
Import failed.Compare your columns with the template examples.
Important noteA file may hold at most 10,000 rows. For items, the stock column becomes both the opening stock and its opening value (at purchase_price); a category is required.

9.11 Assign Codes to Items That Have None

Purpose: Gives a unique code to every item without a barcode.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Print Labels → Assign codes automatically

Procedure:

  1. Open Inventory → Print Labels.
  2. Press Assign codes automatically.

How to verify: The message reads 'Assigned a code to N item(s) that had none.'

9.12 Print Item Labels

Purpose: Prints barcode or QR labels for shelves and products.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Print Labels

Procedure:

  1. Open Inventory → Print Labels.
  2. Choose Code type: Barcode (Code128) or QR code.
  3. Choose Price on label: Show price or Hide price.
  4. Enter the number of Copies beside each item you want.
  5. Press Preview, check it, then press Print.

How to verify: The preview shows one label per copy with the code and, if chosen, the price.

Important noteA barcode scanner types the code into the POS search box, which finds the item.

10. Inventory: stock control and stock reports

Stock adjustments, the movement log, expiring batches and stock valuation.

10.1 Add a Stock Adjustment

Purpose: Changes stock when there was no purchase or sale: found goods, count corrections, damage, samples.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Stock Adjustments → New Adjustment

Procedure:

  1. Choose the Item.
  2. Choose the Type: Stock In, Count Correction (Increase), Stock Out, Damage Write-off, Sample / Free Issue or Count Correction (Decrease).
  3. Enter the Quantity (a whole number) and Date, and an optional Reason.
  4. Choose the Warehouse.
  5. For a batch-tracked item choose the Batch, or for stock coming in type a New Batch No. and Expiry Date.
  6. Press Save Adjustment.

How to verify: The message says stock was increased or reduced by the quantity; the row appears in the table with an IN or OUT badge.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Quantity must be a positive integer.Enter a whole number above zero.
Insufficient stock at this warehouse. Available: NYou cannot take out more than the warehouse holds.
Select which batch this adjustment applies to.Choose a batch.
Item, type, quantity and date are required.Fill them.
Important noteEach adjustment is valued at the item's cost and posted to the accounts, so it affects profit. Choose the type honestly: a shortfall found at a count is a Count Correction, not Damage.

10.2 Reverse a Stock Adjustment

Purpose: Undoes an adjustment that was wrong, restoring stock and cancelling its journal entry.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Inventory → Stock Adjustments → the Reverse button on a row

Procedure:

  1. Find the adjustment in the table.
  2. Press Reverse and confirm.

How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the item's stock returns to what it was.

Important noteA reversal can be refused if undoing it would make stock negative.

10.3 Stock Movements

Purpose: A log of every stock change by warehouse: when, item, warehouse, type, batch, quantity, reference and who.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Stock Movements

Procedure:

  1. Open Inventory → Stock Movements.
  2. Filter by Item, Warehouse and Type and press Filter.
  3. Use Print PDF or Back to Stock Report.

How to verify: A purchase or sale you just made appears as a movement.

10.4 Expiring and Expired Batches

Purpose: Lists batches with their expiry status so you can act before stock is wasted.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Stock Valuation → Expiring Batches, or the Dashboard's Near to Expiry / Expired Items buttons

Procedure:

  1. Open the page.
  2. Filter by Warehouse, Business Category, Status (Expired, Expires Today, Near Expiry, OK, No Expiry Set) and Near-expiry threshold (days).
  3. Press Filter; Clear resets.

How to verify: Each row shows Days to Expiry, Unit Cost and Stock Value.

10.5 Stock Valuation

Purpose: What is in stock now and what it cost: stock, reorder level, average cost, sale price, value and status for each item, plus items below reorder level.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Stock Valuation

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Stock Valuation.
  2. Optionally choose a Warehouse, Business Category or Item; press Clear to reset.
  3. For a batch-tracked item press batches to see each batch.
  4. Use CSV, Excel or Print.

How to verify: With all warehouses selected, the total equals the Inventory line on the Balance Sheet.

Important noteThe page shows stock as it is now; it has no date range.

11. Inventory: warehouse transfers

Moving stock from one warehouse to another. Shown in the menu only when you have more than one warehouse.

11.1 Warehouse Transfers: list and view

Purpose: Lists transfers of stock between warehouses.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Warehouse Transfers (shown only when you have more than one warehouse)

Procedure:

  1. Open the page.
  2. Filter by Status, From (Source) and To (Destination) and press Filter.
  3. Press View on a transfer to open its detail.

How to verify: Each row shows Transfer #, Date, From, To, Items and Status (Draft, Confirmed, Cancelled).

11.2 Create a Warehouse Transfer (Draft)

Purpose: Prepares a list of items to move from one warehouse to another. Nothing moves yet.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Warehouse Transfers → New Transfer

Procedure:

  1. Press New Transfer.
  2. Choose From (Source) and To (Destination) (they must differ) and the Date.
  3. Press + Add Row for each item; choose the Item, enter the Quantity (whole number), and the Batch for a batch-tracked item (or leave it on Auto).
  4. Press Create Draft Transfer.

How to verify: The message says 'Transfer created as Draft. Confirm it to move stock.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Source and destination warehouse must be different.Pick two different warehouses.
A transfer needs at least one item.Add a row.
Quantity must be a whole number.Remove decimals.

11.3 Confirm a Warehouse Transfer

Purpose: Moves the stock out of the source warehouse and into the destination.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Warehouse Transfers → open the transfer → Confirm Transfer

Procedure:

  1. Open the Draft transfer with View.
  2. Press Confirm Transfer.

How to verify: The message says the transfer was confirmed — stock moved; Stock Movements shows the out and in lines.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Insufficient stock — ...The source warehouse does not hold enough; reduce the quantity or add stock first.
Important noteA transfer moves quantity between your own warehouses; it posts no journal entry.

11.4 Cancel a Draft Transfer

Purpose: Abandons a transfer that has not been confirmed.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The transfer's page → Cancel Draft

Procedure:

  1. Open the Draft transfer.
  2. Press Cancel Draft.

How to verify: The message says 'Transfer cancelled.' and the status is Cancelled.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Only a Draft transfer can be cancelled.A confirmed transfer must be reversed instead.

11.5 Reverse a Confirmed Transfer

Purpose: Puts the stock back where it came from.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: The transfer's page → Reverse Transfer

Procedure:

  1. Open the Confirmed transfer.
  2. Press Reverse Transfer and confirm.

How to verify: The message says the transfer was reversed — stock restored.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot reverse — ...The destination no longer holds enough (it was sold or moved on).
This transfer has already been reversed.Nothing more to do.

12. Inventory: physical counts (reconciliation)

Counting stock on the shelf and correcting the system, with a second person's approval.

12.1 Inventory Reconciliation: list and view

Purpose: Lists physical counts against system stock for each warehouse.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Inventory Reconciliation

Procedure:

  1. Open the page.
  2. Filter by Status and Warehouse, press Filter.
  3. Press View on a row.

How to verify: Rows show Reference, Warehouse, Date, Items, Net Variance and Status (Draft, Counted, Approved, Posted, Cancelled).

12.2 Start a Physical Count (New Reconciliation)

Purpose: Opens a count sheet for one warehouse.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Inventory → Inventory Reconciliation → New Reconciliation

Procedure:

  1. Press New Reconciliation.
  2. Choose the Warehouse and Count Date.
  3. Tick Count every active stock item at this warehouse (a full count), or choose specific items for a partial count.
  4. Press Create Reconciliation.

How to verify: The message says it was created as Draft; enter physical counts to continue.

12.3 Enter Physical Counts

Purpose: Records what you actually counted on the shelf.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The reconciliation's page (status Draft)

Procedure:

  1. Open the Draft reconciliation.
  2. Type the Physical Count (a whole number) for every item, with optional Notes.
  3. Press Save Counts.

How to verify: The message says 'Counts saved.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... physical count must be a whole numberRemove decimals or text.
Only a Draft reconciliation accepts counts.Reopen it for recount first.

12.4 Finalize a Count

Purpose: Locks the counts and records the system quantity at that moment so variances can be worked out.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The reconciliation's page → Finalize Count

Procedure:

  1. Make sure every item has a count.
  2. Press Finalize Count.

How to verify: The message says 'Count finalized — system quantities snapshotted.' and the page shows System Qty, Physical Qty and Variance.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Every item needs a physical count before finalizingFill the missing counts.

12.5 Reopen a Count for Recount

Purpose: Returns a finalized count to Draft so counts can be corrected.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The reconciliation's page → Reopen for Recount

Procedure:

  1. Open the Counted reconciliation.
  2. Press Reopen for Recount.

How to verify: The message says 'Reopened for recount.' and the status is Draft.

12.6 Approve a Reconciliation

Purpose: A second person checks the variances before stock is changed.

Who can use it: Admin only, and not the same person who counted.

Where to find it: The reconciliation's page → Approve

Procedure:

  1. Open the Counted reconciliation.
  2. Review Variance for each item.
  3. Press Approve.

How to verify: The message says 'Reconciliation approved.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
The person who counted this reconciliation cannot also approve it.Ask another admin.

12.7 Post a Reconciliation (apply variances)

Purpose: Changes stock to the counted quantities and posts the value difference to the accounts.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: The reconciliation's page → Post — Apply Variances

Procedure:

  1. Open the Approved reconciliation.
  2. Press Post — Apply Variances and confirm.

How to verify: The message says the reconciliation was posted — stock adjusted; Stock Movements shows the changes.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot post — system stock changed since counting ...Stock moved after the count; reopen and recount.
Important notePosting cannot be cancelled afterwards. One journal entry records the net variance against Stock Adjustment.

12.8 Cancel a Reconciliation

Purpose: Abandons a count that has not been posted. No stock moves.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The reconciliation's page → Cancel

Procedure:

  1. Open a Draft or Counted reconciliation.
  2. Press Cancel and confirm.

How to verify: The message says 'Reconciliation cancelled.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... cannot be cancelledAn Approved or Posted reconciliation cannot be cancelled this way.

13. Buying: purchases and suppliers' payments

From ordering to paying: purchase orders, purchase invoices, supplier payments and purchase returns.

13.1 View and Search Purchases

Purpose: Lists purchase invoices with supplier, items, quantity, net total, paid, due, status and date.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases

Procedure:

  1. Open Transactions → Purchases.
  2. Type part of a supplier's name and press Search; press Clear to reset.
  3. Read the Status column: draft, or the payment status Unpaid, Partial or Paid (with a reversed badge if reversed).
  4. Use Print PDF, or Supplier Payments to go to payments.

How to verify: Due equals Net Total minus Paid.

13.2 Create a Purchase (Draft)

Purpose: Records goods bought from a supplier. It is saved as a Draft; nothing is booked until you post it.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases → New Purchase Invoice

Procedure:

  1. Press Select Supplier and choose the supplier.
  2. Check the Date and choose Received Into (Warehouse). Add optional Notes.
  3. For each item: press Select Item…; choose the Unit if the item has more than one; enter Qty and Price / Unit; optionally a Discount (% or Fixed) and Tax (%).
  4. For a batch-tracked item fill Batch No and Expiry Date.
  5. Press Add Item for more lines (× removes a line).
  6. Press Save Purchase.

How to verify: The message reads 'Draft Purchase #N saved.' and the row shows draft. Stock and the supplier's ledger have not changed yet.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Supplier and date are required!Choose a supplier and a date.
At least one item is required!Add a line with an item and quantity.
Access denied. Only managers and admins can add purchases.Ask a manager or admin.
Important notePrices and amounts shown here are illustrations of the form only; no real purchase exists until you save one.

13.3 Edit a Draft Purchase

Purpose: Corrects a purchase before it is posted.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases → the pencil button (Edit purchase)

Procedure:

  1. Press the pencil button on a draft row.
  2. Change supplier, date, notes or lines.
  3. Press Update Purchase (or Cancel).

How to verify: The message says the purchase was updated successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Purchase #N is posted to the general ledger and can no longer be changed. Reverse it instead.A posted purchase cannot be edited: an admin can Correct or Reverse it.
Supplier and date are required!Fill them.
At least one item is required!Keep one line.

13.4 Post a Purchase

Purpose: Turns a Draft into a real purchase: stock goes up, an invoice number is assigned, the supplier ledger and the books are updated.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases → the green tick (Post) on a draft row

Procedure:

  1. Check the draft is correct (open Edit purchase if unsure).
  2. Press the green tick button.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Purchase <invoice number> posted successfully!'; the row gets an invoice number and a payment status; the item's stock has risen in the chosen warehouse.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Purchase #N is not a Draft — nothing to post.It is already posted.
Important noteAfter posting it cannot be edited or deleted; only an admin can correct or reverse it. Stock is valued at the goods cost without tax.

13.5 Delete a Draft Purchase

Purpose: Throws away a draft that is not needed. It has no stock or accounting effect.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases → the trash button on a draft row

Procedure:

  1. Press the trash button.
  2. Confirm 'Delete this Draft Purchase?'.

How to verify: The message says the purchase was deleted successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete purchase with associated payments! Delete payments first.Reverse the payments first.
Cannot delete purchase — it was created from Purchase Order #N.Leave it, or reverse it if it is posted.
Important noteA posted or numbered purchase can never be deleted; reverse it.

13.6 View and Print a Purchase Invoice

Purpose: Opens the invoice as a printable page.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases → the receipt button (View invoice)

Procedure:

  1. Press View invoice on the row; it opens in a new tab.
  2. Press the green print button, then print or save as PDF in the browser window.

How to verify: The invoice shows the supplier, lines and totals. A reversed purchase prints with a REVERSED watermark.

13.7 Correct a Posted Purchase

Purpose: Fixes a mistake in a posted purchase in one step instead of reversing and re-entering it.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases → the amber shield button (Correct this Posted Purchase)

Procedure:

  1. Press the shield button on a posted row.
  2. Compare the Original Purchase with Corrected Purchase Details; change supplier, notes or lines.
  3. Type the Reason for correction.
  4. If the purchase has active payments, tick the confirmation that they remain unchanged.
  5. Press Apply Correction.

How to verify: The message says the purchase was corrected successfully; the audit log records the reason.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
A reason for this correction is required.Type the reason.
Cannot correct — insufficient stock to undo the original receipt: ...Some of the goods were already sold; correct or reverse the sale first.
... has N Purchase Return(s) recordedA purchase with returns cannot be corrected; reverse the returns first.
Important noteExisting payments stay as they are; if the corrected total is lower than what was paid the difference becomes supplier credit.

13.8 Reverse a Purchase

Purpose: Cancels a posted purchase that should not have existed, keeping both the original and the cancelling entry on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchases → Reverse on a posted row

Procedure:

  1. Find the purchase in the list.
  2. Press Reverse on its row.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the message says the purchase was reversed with a journal entry number.

Important noteStock goes back down, the supplier ledger is corrected and a cancelling journal entry is posted. If the purchase has active payments you get an extra warning and must confirm; the payments stay recorded and may leave a supplier credit. It can be refused if the goods were already sold.

13.9 Create a Purchase Order

Purpose: Records an order placed with a supplier. It moves no stock and posts nothing.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchase Orders → New Purchase Order

Procedure:

  1. Choose the Supplier and Order Date; optionally Expected Delivery and Notes.
  2. Press + Add Row for each item; choose item, unit, Qty, Purchase Price, discount and tax.
  3. Press Create PO.

How to verify: The message reads 'Purchase Order #N created.' and it appears in the list with its status.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Supplier and order date are required.Fill both.
At least one item is required.Add a row.

13.10 View a Purchase Order and Change Its Status

Purpose: Tracks an order through Draft, Confirmed, Received or Cancelled.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchase Orders → View

Procedure:

  1. Press View on an order.
  2. Pick a new status in the status box and press Update Status.
  3. Use Print PDF or Back.

How to verify: The message reads 'PO #N status updated to ...'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot change status of a Received or Cancelled order.Those two are final.

13.11 Convert a Purchase Order to a Purchase

Purpose: Turns an order into a real, posted purchase when the goods arrive.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The purchase order's page → Convert to Purchase

Procedure:

  1. Open the order.
  2. Set the purchase_date (the date field next to the button).
  3. Press Convert to Purchase.

How to verify: The message says the PO was converted to Purchase <invoice number> successfully; the order shows Received.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cancelled orders cannot be converted.Create a new order.
Already converted to Purchase #N.Open that purchase instead.
PO has no line items.Add items first.
Important noteThe purchase is created already posted, so stock and the supplier ledger change at once. The page has no warehouse box, so the goods go into the default warehouse.

13.12 Delete a Purchase Order

Purpose: Removes an order that is no longer wanted.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: The purchase order's page → Delete PO

Procedure:

  1. Open the order.
  2. Press Delete PO and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Purchase Order #N deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete a received order.A received order stays as a record.

13.13 Record a Supplier Payment

Purpose: Records money paid to a supplier, optionally against a specific purchase.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Supplier Payments → Record Payment

Procedure:

  1. Press Select Supplier and choose the supplier.
  2. Optionally choose Against Purchase, or leave General / On Account.
  3. Enter the Amount and Payment Date.
  4. Choose the Method and the Account the money left from.
  5. Optionally enter Reference No. and Notes.
  6. Press Save Payment.

How to verify: The message says the payment was recorded successfully; it appears in the table; the supplier's balance falls.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Supplier, amount and payment date are required!Fill them.
Payment exceeds purchase balance due (...)!Pay no more than what is due on that purchase.
Selected purchase is still a Draft and has no payable balance yet — post it first.Post the purchase first.
Invalid payment method!Choose one of Cash, Bank, Cheque, Online.
Important notePick the right Account: it decides which cash or bank balance falls and what the Cash Flow and Balance Sheet show.

13.14 Edit a Supplier Payment

Status: A payment is posted to the books the moment it is saved, and the code refuses to change a posted payment. Pressing Edit is therefore expected to show 'Payment #N is posted to the general ledger and can no longer be changed. Reverse it instead.' (read from the code, not tried in the browser).

Purpose: The list offers an Edit button on each payment.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Supplier Payments → pencil button (Edit)

Procedure:

  1. To correct a payment: Reverse it (next box), then record it again correctly.

How to verify: The corrected payment shows in the list; the wrong one shows reversed.

13.15 Reverse a Supplier Payment

Purpose: Cancels a posted supplier payment that should not have existed, keeping both the original and the cancelling entry on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Supplier Payments → Reverse on the row

Procedure:

  1. Find the supplier payment in the list.
  2. Press Reverse on its row.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.
