How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the message says the supplier payment was reversed with a journal entry number.

Important noteThe payment is cancelled, the supplier's balance goes back up and the cash or bank balance is restored.

13.16 Pay Several Purchases at Once (Bulk Payment)

Purpose: Pays a supplier's open bills in one go, optionally splitting a total amount automatically.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Bulk Payment (or Bulk Payment on the Supplier Payments page)

Procedure:

  1. Choose the Supplier.
  2. Optionally type a Total Payment Amount and press Load & Auto-Distribute; or press Pay Full Balance to fill every bill's balance.
  3. Adjust Pay Now against each bill (PUR #, Date, Invoice Total, Already Paid, Balance Due); the general box takes any remainder on account.
  4. Enter Payment Date, Payment Method, the Account, optional Reference / Cheque # and Notes.
  5. Press Save Bulk Payment (Clear All empties the boxes).

How to verify: One payment per bill appears on the Supplier Payments page.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Please enter at least one payment amount.Fill at least one Pay Now box.
Supplier and payment date are required!Choose both.

13.17 Record a Purchase Return

Purpose: Sends goods back to a supplier against an original purchase line.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchase Returns

Procedure:

  1. Open Transactions → Purchase Returns and find Record Purchase Return.
  2. Choose the Return Date.
  3. For each line press — Select Purchase / Item — and pick the purchase line; enter Qty, Return Price and an optional Reason.
  4. Press Add Item for more lines.
  5. Press Record Returns.

How to verify: The message reads 'N purchase return(s) recorded successfully!'; the returns table lists them and stock falls.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Row N (...): cannot return X, only Y remaining.You cannot return more than was bought, less what was already returned.
Row N: quantity must be a positive integer.Whole numbers above zero.
Row N: return price cannot be negative.Enter zero or more.
Date is required!Choose a date.
Important noteUse a return when goods physically went back. Use a reversal when the document itself was a mistake.

13.18 Reverse a Purchase Return

Purpose: Cancels a posted purchase return that should not have existed, keeping both the original and the cancelling entry on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Purchase Returns → Reverse on the row

Procedure:

  1. Find the purchase return in the list.
  2. Press Reverse on its row.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the message says the purchase return was reversed with a journal entry number.

Important noteThe return is cancelled and its stock and ledger effects are undone.

14. Selling: sales and customers' receipts

Quotations, sales invoices, delivery challans, customer receipts and sale returns. The counter POS has its own section.

14.1 View and Search Sales

Purpose: Lists sales invoices with customer, items, quantity, net total, received, due, status and date.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales

Procedure:

  1. Open Transactions → Sales.
  2. Search by customer name; Clear resets.
  3. Read Status: draft, or Unpaid, Partial or Paid.
  4. Use Print PDF or Customer Receipts.

How to verify: Due equals Net Total minus Received.

14.2 Create a Sale (Draft)

Purpose: Records goods sold to a customer. It is saved as a Draft; stock and accounts change only when you post it.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales → New Sale Invoice

Procedure:

  1. Press Select Customer and choose the customer.
  2. Check the Date, choose the Warehouse the goods leave from, and add optional Notes.
  3. For each item press Select Item…; choose Unit; enter Qty and Sale Price; watch the Stock column; optionally a Discount (% or Fixed) and Tax (%).
  4. Press Add Item for more lines (× removes one).
  5. Press Save Sale.

How to verify: The message reads 'Draft Sale #N saved.' and the row shows draft; stock has not moved yet.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Customer and date are required!Choose both.
At least one item is required!Add a line.
Insufficient stock — ...The chosen warehouse holds less than you entered; lower the quantity or choose another warehouse.
Access denied. Only managers and admins can add sales.Ask a manager or admin.
Important noteYou cannot sell more than the warehouse holds. Amounts in this manual are examples only.

14.3 Edit a Draft Sale

Purpose: Corrects a sale before it is posted.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales → pencil button (Edit sale) on a draft row

Procedure:

  1. Press the pencil button.
  2. Change customer, date, notes or lines.
  3. Press Update Sale (or Cancel).

How to verify: The message says the sale was updated successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Sale #N is posted to the general ledger and can no longer be changed. Reverse it instead.Posted sales cannot be edited; an admin can Correct or Reverse.
Insufficient stock — ...Lower the quantity.

14.4 Post a Sale

Purpose: Turns a Draft into a real sale: stock goes down (batches by earliest expiry first), the invoice number is assigned, the customer ledger and the books are updated, and the cost of goods sold is recorded.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales → the green tick (Post) on a draft row

Procedure:

  1. Check the draft.
  2. Press the green tick button.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Sale <invoice number> posted successfully!'; the row has an invoice number and a payment status; stock fell.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot post — insufficient stock: ...Stock changed since you saved the draft; reduce quantities or receive stock.
Sale #N is not a Draft — nothing to post.Already posted.
Important noteAfter posting it cannot be edited or deleted; only an admin can correct or reverse it.

14.5 Delete a Draft Sale

Purpose: Discards a draft that is not needed. No stock or accounting effect.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales → trash button on a draft row

Procedure:

  1. Press the trash button.
  2. Confirm 'Delete this Draft Sale?'.

How to verify: The message says the sale was deleted successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete sale with associated receipts! Delete receipts first.Reverse the receipts first.
Cannot delete sale — it was created from Quotation #N.Keep it.

14.6 View and Print a Sale Invoice

Purpose: Opens the invoice as a printable page to give to the customer.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales → receipt button (View invoice)

Procedure:

  1. Press View invoice; it opens in a new tab.
  2. Press the green print button and print or save as PDF.

How to verify: The invoice shows customer, lines and totals; a reversed sale prints with a REVERSED watermark.

14.7 Correct a Posted Sale

Purpose: Fixes a mistake in a posted sale in one step.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales → amber shield button (Correct this Posted Sale)

Procedure:

  1. Press the shield button on a posted row.
  2. Compare Original Sale with Corrected Sale Details and change customer, notes or lines.
  3. Type the Reason for correction.
  4. If the sale has receipts, tick the confirmation that they remain unchanged and any shortfall becomes customer credit.
  5. Press Apply Correction.

How to verify: The message says the sale was corrected successfully.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
A reason for this correction is required.Type the reason.
Cannot correct — insufficient stock: ...There is not enough stock for the corrected lines.
... has N Sale Return(s) recorded against itReverse the returns first.

14.8 Reverse a Sale

Purpose: Cancels a posted sale that should not have existed, keeping both the original and the cancelling entry on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sales → Reverse on a posted row

Procedure:

  1. Find the sale in the list.
  2. Press Reverse on its row.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the message says the sale was reversed with a journal entry number.

Important noteStock goes back up, the customer ledger is corrected and a cancelling journal entry is posted. If the sale has receipts you get an extra warning and must confirm; the receipts stay and may leave a customer credit.

14.9 Create a Quotation

Purpose: Prepares a price offer for a customer. It moves no stock and posts nothing.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Quotations → New Quotation

Procedure:

  1. Choose the Customer and Quote Date; optionally Valid Until and Notes.
  2. Press + Add Row for each item; choose item, unit, Qty, Sale Price, discount type and value, and tax %.
  3. Press Create Quotation.

How to verify: The message reads 'Quotation #N created.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Customer and date are required.Fill both.
At least one item is required.Add a row.

14.10 Edit a Quotation

Purpose: Changes a quotation before it is converted.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The quotation's page → Edit

Procedure:

  1. Open the quotation with View, then press Edit.
  2. Change the fields or rows.
  3. Press Save Changes (or Cancel).

How to verify: The message reads 'Quotation #N updated.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Converted quotations cannot be edited.Edit the resulting sale instead, if it is still a draft.

14.11 Change a Quotation's Status

Purpose: Tracks a quotation as Draft, Sent, Accepted or Rejected.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The quotation's page → status box → Update

Procedure:

  1. Open the quotation.
  2. Pick a status and press Update.

How to verify: The message reads 'Quotation #N marked as ...'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Converted quotations cannot be changed.A converted quotation is final.

14.12 Convert a Quotation to a Sale

Purpose: Turns an accepted quotation into a real, posted sale.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: The quotation's page → Convert to Sale

Procedure:

  1. Open the quotation.
  2. Set sale_date and choose the Warehouse (location).
  3. Press Convert to Sale.

How to verify: The message says the quotation was converted to Sale <invoice number>; its status is Converted.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot convert a rejected or already-converted quotation.Create a new quotation.
Insufficient stock — ...The warehouse does not hold enough of an item.
Already converted to Sale #N.Open that sale.
Important noteThe sale is created already posted, so stock, the customer ledger and the books change at once.

14.13 Delete a Quotation

Purpose: Removes a quotation that is not needed.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: The quotation's page → Delete

Procedure:

  1. Open the quotation.
  2. Press Delete and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Quotation #N deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cannot delete a converted quotation.It is linked to a sale.

14.14 Create a Delivery Challan

Purpose: Records the dispatch of an invoiced sale. It posts nothing and moves no stock.

Who can use it: Managers and admins create; any verified user can view the list.

Where to find it: Transactions → Delivery Challans

Procedure:

  1. Open Transactions → Delivery Challans.
  2. Pick the Sale Invoice from the list, and the Challan Date.
  3. Optionally type Transport / Carrier and Notes.
  4. Press Create Challan.

How to verify: The message reads 'Delivery Challan #N created.' and it appears with status Pending.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Sale and challan date are required.Fill both.
A challan already exists for this sale.Update the existing one.

14.15 Update a Delivery Challan

Purpose: Tracks a challan as Pending, Dispatched, Delivered or Cancelled and records dates and transport.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Delivery Challans → Update column

Procedure:

  1. Open the update panel for a challan (Update DC #N).
  2. Change Status, Transport, Dispatch Date, Delivery Date or notes.
  3. Press Save (or Cancel).

How to verify: The message reads 'Challan #N updated.'

14.16 Record a Customer Receipt

Purpose: Records money received from a customer, optionally against a specific sale.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Customer Receipts → Record Receipt

Procedure:

  1. Press Select Customer and choose the customer.
  2. Optionally choose Against Sale, or leave General / On Account.
  3. Enter the Amount and Receipt Date.
  4. Choose the Method and the Account the money went into.
  5. Optionally enter Reference No. and Notes.
  6. Press Save Receipt.

How to verify: The message says the receipt was recorded successfully; the customer's balance falls.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Customer, amount and receipt date are required!Fill them.
Receipt exceeds sale balance due (...)!Receive no more than is due on that sale.
Selected sale is still a Draft and has no receivable balance yet — post it first.Post the sale first.
Invalid payment method!Choose Cash, Bank, Cheque or Online.

14.17 Edit a Customer Receipt

Status: A receipt is posted to the books when saved, and the code refuses to change a posted receipt, so Edit is expected to show 'Payment #N is posted to the general ledger and can no longer be changed. Reverse it instead.' (read from the code, not tried in the browser).

Purpose: The list offers an Edit button on each receipt.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Customer Receipts → pencil button (Edit)

Procedure:

  1. To correct a receipt: Reverse it (next box), then record it again correctly.

How to verify: The corrected receipt appears; the wrong one shows reversed.

14.18 Reverse a Customer Receipt

Purpose: Cancels a posted customer receipt that should not have existed, keeping both the original and the cancelling entry on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Customer Receipts → Reverse on the row

Procedure:

  1. Find the customer receipt in the list.
  2. Press Reverse on its row.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the message says the customer receipt was reversed with a journal entry number.

Important noteThe receipt is cancelled, the customer's balance goes back up and the cash or bank balance falls.

14.19 Receive Against Several Sales at Once (Bulk Receipt)

Purpose: Records a customer's payment across several open invoices, optionally splitting a total automatically.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Bulk Receipt (or Bulk Receipt on the Customer Receipts page)

Procedure:

  1. Choose the Customer.
  2. Optionally type Total Receipt Amount and press Load & Auto-Distribute, or press Receive Full Balance.
  3. Adjust Receive Now against each invoice (SAL #, Date, Invoice Total, Already Received, Balance Due); the general box takes any remainder on account.
  4. Enter Receipt Date, Payment Method, the Account, optional Reference / Cheque # and Notes.
  5. Press Save Bulk Receipt (Clear All empties the boxes).

How to verify: One receipt per invoice appears on the Customer Receipts page.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Please enter at least one receipt amount.Fill at least one Receive Now box.
Customer and receipt date are required!Choose both.

14.20 Record a Sale Return

Purpose: Takes goods back from a customer against an original sale line, at the cost they left at.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sale Returns

Procedure:

  1. Open Transactions → Sale Returns and find Record Sale Return.
  2. Choose the Return Date.
  3. For each line press — Select Sale / Item — and pick the sale line; enter Qty, Return Price and an optional Reason.
  4. Press Add Item for more lines.
  5. Press Record Returns.

How to verify: The message reads 'N sale return(s) recorded successfully!'; stock rises and the customer's balance falls.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Row N (...): cannot return X, only Y remaining.You cannot return more than was sold, less what was already returned.
Row N: quantity must be a positive integer.Whole numbers above zero.
Date is required!Choose a date.
Important noteA draft or reversed sale cannot be returned against.

14.21 Reverse a Sale Return

Purpose: Cancels a posted sale return that should not have existed, keeping both the original and the cancelling entry on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Sale Returns → Reverse on the row

Procedure:

  1. Find the sale return in the list.
  2. Press Reverse on its row.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the message says the sale return was reversed with a journal entry number.

Important noteThe return is cancelled and its stock and ledger effects are undone.

15. Point of Sale (POS)

A fast counter screen for managers and admins. Unlike a normal sale, a POS sale is posted immediately.

15.1 Ring Up a POS Sale

Purpose: Sells items at the counter, takes payment, and posts the sale at once.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: POS in the menu

Procedure:

  1. Open POS and check the Warehouse the goods leave from.
  2. Click the search box (Scan barcode / QR, or type an item name), scan or type, and add the item; repeat for each item.
  3. Press Walk-in Customer to choose another customer if needed.
  4. Tap the Payment method button of the cash or bank account the money goes into.
  5. Enter Amount received; the Change line shows what to give back.
  6. Press Complete Sale.

How to verify: The receipt opens for printing; stock fell in the chosen warehouse; the sale appears in Sales with its invoice number.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
The cart is empty.Add an item first.
Only N × <item> available ...That warehouse holds less than the cart asks for.
Invalid amount paid.Type a number.
Bad quantity or price for <item>.Fix that cart line.
Important noteIf you enter less than the total, the amount received is recorded and the rest stays due on the customer — including the Walk-in Customer — so enter the full amount for a cash sale. Clear Cart empties the screen without selling.

15.2 Choose a Batch at the POS

Purpose: For batch-tracked items, lets the cashier pick which batch is sold instead of the automatic earliest-expiry choice.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: POS → the Batch: Auto (FEFO) link under a batch-tracked item in the cart

Procedure:

  1. Add a batch-tracked item to the cart.
  2. Press Batch: Auto (FEFO) under it.
  3. Pick the batch(es) and quantities from the list; expired or near-expiry batches are tagged.
  4. Continue with the sale.

How to verify: The cart line shows 'Batch: <number>' instead of Auto (FEFO).

Important noteIf you do nothing, the system takes stock from the batch that expires first.

15.3 Hold a Bill

Purpose: Sets the current cart aside, for example when another customer needs serving.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: POS → Hold Bill

Procedure:

  1. Build the cart.
  2. Press Hold Bill.
  3. The cart clears and the bill is saved under a hold number.

How to verify: The bill appears in Held Bills.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Cart is empty.Add items first.
Customer required.Choose a customer (the Walk-in Customer counts).
Hold was updated by another user. Please reload and try again.Reload the held bill.
Important noteA held bill is not a sale: no stock moves and nothing is posted until you complete it.

15.4 Resume a Held Bill

Purpose: Brings a held bill back into the POS cart.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Held Bills in the menu (or Held Bills on the POS screen)

Procedure:

  1. Open Held Bills.
  2. Search by customer or hold number, or sort by Most Recent, Customer Name or Total Amount.
  3. Press Resume on the bill.
  4. Finish with Complete Sale.

How to verify: The POS cart shows the same customer and items.

15.5 Delete a Held Bill

Purpose: Discards a held bill that will not be used.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Held Bills → Delete

Procedure:

  1. Open Held Bills.
  2. Press Delete on the bill and confirm.

How to verify: The bill disappears from the list.

15.6 Print a POS Receipt

Purpose: Prints the receipt for a completed POS sale on 80 mm paper.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: It opens after Complete Sale; the Print button is on the receipt page.

Procedure:

  1. After Complete Sale the receipt page opens.
  2. Press Print and choose the receipt printer.
  3. Back to POS returns to the counter.

How to verify: A narrow 80 mm slip prints with the items, totals and, where set, a barcode.

Important noteThe sale page address ends in /pos/receipt/<number>; adding ?format=legacy shows the older wider layout.

16. Expenses

Running costs such as rent and electricity.

16.1 Record an Expense

Purpose: Records money spent on running the business.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Expenses

Procedure:

  1. Type the Description (an example: Office Rent).
  2. Enter the Amount and Date.
  3. Choose the Category (it decides which account the cost goes to).
  4. Choose the Payment Method and the Account the money left from.
  5. Optionally enter Reference # and Notes.
  6. Press Save Expense.

How to verify: The message reads 'Expense recorded.' and the row appears in the table; the Profit & Loss includes it.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Description, amount and date are required.Fill them.
Amount must be positive.Enter more than zero.
Invalid payment method!Choose one of the four.
Invalid amount or date.Correct the format.
Important noteChoose the right Account; it decides which cash or bank balance falls.

16.2 Add an Expense Category

Purpose: Creates a category and chooses which ledger account it posts to.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Transactions → Expenses → the category box (New category name)

Procedure:

  1. Type the New category name.
  2. Choose the ledger account in the box beside it (the default is 6090 Other Expenses).
  3. Press Add.

How to verify: The message reads 'Category '...' added.' and it appears in the Category list of the expense form.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Category name is required.Type a name.
Category already exists.Use the existing one.

16.3 Reverse a Expense

Purpose: Cancels a posted expense that should not have existed, keeping both the original and the cancelling entry on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Transactions → Expenses → Reverse on the row

Procedure:

  1. Find the expense in the list.
  2. Press Reverse on its row.
  3. Read the confirmation and confirm.

How to verify: The row shows a reversed badge and the message says the expense was reversed with a journal entry number.

Important noteThe expense is cancelled and the cash or bank balance is restored. The Expenses page has no edit button: to correct an expense, reverse it and record it again.

17. Accounting

Chart of accounts, cash and bank balances, journal entries, fixed assets, tax codes and fiscal years. Menu: Accounting.

17.1 View the Chart of Accounts

Purpose: Lists every ledger account with code, type, flags and balance.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Chart of Accounts

Procedure:

  1. Open Accounting → Chart of Accounts.
  2. Pick a date in Balances as of and press Show to see balances on that day.
  3. Use Print PDF, or the buttons Tax Codes and Dashboard.

How to verify: Accounts the system posts to carry a system flag.

17.2 Add a Ledger Account

Purpose: Adds an account such as a new expense.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Accounting → Chart of Accounts → New Account

Procedure:

  1. Press New Account.
  2. Enter the Code and Name (an example: 6050, Internet & Broadband).
  3. Optionally choose a Parent (main account).
  4. Choose the Type.
  5. Optionally set the Cash flow section.
  6. Tick This is a main account (a heading) only for a heading that nothing is posted to.
  7. Press Create Account.

How to verify: The message reads 'Account <code> — <name> created.' and it appears in the chart.

17.3 Edit a Ledger Account

Purpose: Renames an account, moves it under another, or makes it inactive.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Accounting → Chart of Accounts → pencil button (Edit)

Procedure:

  1. Press the pencil button.
  2. Change Name, Parent, Cash flow section or the Active tick.
  3. Press Save.

How to verify: The message reads 'Account <code> — <name> updated.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
An account cannot be its own parent.Choose a different parent.
This account has journal entries; it cannot be deactivated.Keep it active.
This heading still has active sub-accounts.Deactivate the sub-accounts first.

17.4 Delete a Ledger Account

Purpose: Removes an account that was added by mistake and never used.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Accounting → Chart of Accounts → Edit → Delete this account

Procedure:

  1. Open the account with the pencil button.
  2. Press Delete this account and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Account <code> — <name> deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... is used by the posting layer and cannot be deleted.A system account; leave it.
... has journal entries. Deactivate it instead.Edit it and untick Active.
... still has sub-accounts.Remove or move them first.
... belongs to a cash/bank account / is used by an expense category / is used by a tax code.Change that link first.

17.5 Tax Codes: view and change rates

Purpose: Shows each tax component with its rate and input and output accounts, and lets you change the rates.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Accounting → Tax Codes (also a button on the Chart of Accounts)

Procedure:

  1. Open Accounting → Tax Codes.
  2. Change the figure in a Rate % box.
  3. Press Save Rates.

How to verify: The message reads 'Tax rates updated.'

Important noteOnly admins can change tax rates. Check the rates before you post documents that carry tax.

17.6 View Fiscal Years and Periods

Purpose: Shows each fiscal year and its months with open or closed status. Nothing can be posted on a date outside an open period.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fiscal Years & Periods

Procedure:

  1. Open Accounting → Fiscal Years & Periods.
  2. Read the Period, From, To, Status columns.
  3. Use Print PDF.

How to verify: The month containing today is listed and open.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
No fiscal year covers <date>. Create the fiscal year first.An admin must create the fiscal year.
<period> is closed. Post to an open period.Use a date in an open month, or ask an admin to reopen it.

17.7 Create a Fiscal Year

Purpose: Creates a year with its twelve monthly periods.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fiscal Years & Periods → Create fiscal year

Procedure:

  1. Type the year number (an example: 2027).
  2. Press Create.

How to verify: The message reads 'Fiscal year <year> created with 12 periods.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Enter a valid year.Type a four-digit year.
Fiscal year <year> already exists.Nothing to do.

17.8 Close or Reopen a Period

Purpose: Locks a month so its figures cannot change, or unlocks it.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fiscal Years & Periods → Close / Reopen in the Action column

Procedure:

  1. Find the month.
  2. Press Close (or Reopen for a closed month).

How to verify: The message reads '<period> closed.' or '<period> reopened.' and the Status changes.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Fiscal year ... is closed; its periods cannot be reopened individually.Once a year is closed, its months stay closed.

17.9 Close a Fiscal Year

Purpose: Moves the year's profit or loss into Retained Earnings and locks every period of that year.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fiscal Years & Periods → Close Year

Procedure:

  1. Finish the month-end checks (see Month End).
  2. Download a backup.
  3. Press Close Year and read the confirmation.
  4. Confirm.

How to verify: The message says the year was closed and how much profit or loss moved to Retained Earnings.

Important noteThis cannot be undone from the page. Invoice numbering restarts in the new year.

17.10 Cash & Bank Accounts: view balances

Purpose: Shows each cash or bank account with opening and current balance.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Cash & Bank Accounts

Procedure:

  1. Open Accounting → Cash & Bank Accounts.
  2. Read Account, Type, Opening and Current Balance.
  3. Press Manage Accounts (admins) to add or edit accounts; Print PDF to print.

How to verify: The balances equal the matching cash and bank lines on the Balance Sheet.

Important noteA balance is always read from the general ledger, so it includes every journal entry posted to that account, not only receipts, payments and expenses.

17.11 Cash & Bank Account Ledger

Purpose: Shows every movement in one account: date, description, in, out and balance.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Cash & Bank Accounts → the ledger button on a row

Procedure:

  1. Press the ledger button on the account.
  2. Read the lines; press Back to Accounts to return.

How to verify: The last Balance equals the account's Current Balance.

17.12 View Journal Entries

Purpose: Lists recent journal entries and a summary of debits and credits per account.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Journal Entries

Procedure:

  1. Open Accounting → Journal Entries.
  2. Read Recent Entries (#, Date, Source, Reference, Description, Amount) and Account Summary (Total Debit, Total Credit, Net).
  3. Press an entry to open it.

How to verify: Every sale, purchase, payment and expense you post has an entry here.

17.13 Post a Manual Journal Entry

Purpose: Records something no document covers, such as an owner putting money in, a bank charge or an accrual.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Journal Entries → New Entry

Procedure:

  1. Press New Entry.
  2. Enter the Date, an optional Reference and a Description (an example: Depreciation for the month).
  3. On each line choose the Account and enter either a Debit or a Credit; press Add Line for more.
  4. Make total debits equal total credits.
  5. Press Post Entry.

How to verify: The message reads 'Journal entry #N posted.' and it appears in the list.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Entry is out of balance: debits X ≠ credits Y.Make both totals equal.
... is a control account — it is maintained by its subledger and cannot be posted to by hand.Receivables, Payables and Inventory move only through documents.
<period> is closed. Post to an open period.Use an open month.
A journal entry needs a description.Type a description.
A journal entry needs at least two lines with amounts.Add the second line.
Important notePost only what no normal screen can record. Receivables, Payables and Inventory are control accounts: they move only through documents, never by hand. A manual entry cannot be edited afterwards; it can only be reversed.

17.14 View a Journal Entry

Purpose: Shows the lines of one entry: code, account, debit and credit.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Journal Entries → open an entry

Procedure:

  1. Open an entry from the list.
  2. Press Print or Back.

How to verify: Total debits equal total credits.

17.15 Reverse a Journal Entry

Purpose: Posts a mirror entry that cancels a wrong one; both stay on record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: An entry's page → Reverse this entry

Procedure:

  1. Open the entry.
  2. Press Reverse this entry and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Entry #N reversed by #M. Both remain in the ledger.'

Important noteAn entry cannot be reversed twice, and a reversal cannot itself be reversed. Entries made by documents (sales, purchases and so on) are reversed from the document, not here.

17.16 Fixed Asset Register

Purpose: Lists assets with acquisition date, method, cost, accumulated depreciation, net book value and status.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fixed Assets

Procedure:

  1. Open Accounting → Fixed Assets.
  2. Press an asset to open it; Print PDF prints the register.

How to verify: Net book value is cost minus accumulated depreciation.

17.17 Add a Fixed Asset

Purpose: Records something bought to use, such as a van or computer, and posts the purchase.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fixed Assets → New Asset

Procedure:

  1. Press New Asset.
  2. Enter Asset name (an example: Delivery Van), optional Tag / Serial, Acquired on and Cost.
  3. Enter Salvage value if any, and choose Paid from (the cash or bank account).
  4. Choose the Method and fill Useful life (months) (Straight Line) or Rate (% per year) (Reducing Balance).
  5. Press Save & Post.

How to verify: The message reads 'Fixed asset '<name>' recorded and posted.' Cash or bank falls by the cost; it is an asset, not an expense.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
The asset needs a name.Type a name.
Cost must be greater than zero.Enter the cost.
Salvage value must be zero or more, and less than the cost.Correct the salvage value.
Straight Line needs a useful life in months.Enter the months.
Reducing Balance needs a yearly rate.Enter the rate.
Choose the account that paid for the asset.Choose Paid from.

17.18 Run Monthly Depreciation

Purpose: Charges one month of depreciation on every eligible asset in a single journal entry.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fixed Assets → Run Monthly Depreciation

Procedure:

  1. Choose the Month.
  2. Press Post Depreciation.

How to verify: The message reads 'Depreciation for <month year> posted — <amount>'. Each asset's page lists the charge.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Pick the month to depreciate.Choose a month.
Nothing to depreciate for <Month Year>.That month was already posted or no asset is eligible.
Important noteA month can be posted only once. Do it once at month end.

17.19 View a Fixed Asset

Purpose: Shows one asset's monthly charges: month, charge, accumulated and net book value.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Accounting → Fixed Assets → open an asset

Procedure:

  1. Open an asset.
  2. Read the table; Print prints it; Back returns.

How to verify: Each row links to its journal entry.

17.20 Dispose of a Fixed Asset

Purpose: Records selling or scrapping an asset and works out the gain or loss against its book value.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: An asset's page → Disposal date / Proceeds / Money received into → Dispose

Procedure:

  1. Open the asset.
  2. Enter the Disposal date and the Proceeds (zero if scrapped).
  3. Choose Money received into (leave '— none (scrapped) —' if nothing came in).
  4. Press Dispose.

How to verify: The message says the asset was disposed with the outcome (gain or loss) and a journal entry number.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Check the disposal date and the proceeds.Fix the numbers.
Proceeds cannot be negative.Enter zero or more.
Choose the account the money was received into.Choose an account when proceeds are above zero.

18. Reports

Every report is menu Reports (managers and admins). Statements are added up from the general ledger.

18.1 Sales & Inventory Reports: summary

Purpose: Shows, for a date range, Gross Sale, Sale Returns, Net Sales, Purchase Cost (COGS), Purchase Returns, Net Purchase Cost, Gross Profit and Profit Margin.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Sales & Inventory

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Sales & Inventory.
  2. Choose Start Date and End Date.
  3. Press Generate Reports.
  4. Read the Metric / Amount table; use Print.

How to verify: Net Sales equals Gross Sale minus Sale Returns.

Important noteGross Sale and Net Sales are different figures: Net Sales is after sale returns. Read the label before quoting a number.

18.2 Profit Reports (by date, item, category, customer)

Purpose: Shows Sale Amount and Profit grouped by date, by item, by category and by customer.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Sales & Inventory → Profit Reports tab (after Generate Reports)

Procedure:

  1. Generate the report for your dates.
  2. Press the Profit Reports tab.
  3. Read the four tables; each has a Total row and its own CSV and Excel buttons.

How to verify: The Total of each table equals the Gross Sale figure.

18.3 Purchase Reports (by supplier, lines, payments)

Purpose: Shows purchases per supplier (bills, total quantity, total amount), every purchase line, and supplier payments.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Sales & Inventory → Purchases tab

Procedure:

  1. Generate the report for your dates.
  2. Press the Purchases tab.
  3. Read the three tables; use their CSV or Excel buttons.

How to verify: Pick one bill on the Purchases page and find it in the lines table.

18.4 Sales Reports (lines and receipts)

Purpose: Shows every sale line and every customer receipt in the range.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Sales & Inventory → Sales tab

Procedure:

  1. Generate the report for your dates.
  2. Press the Sales tab.
  3. Read the tables; use CSV or Excel.

How to verify: Pick one sale on the Sales page and find it in the lines table.

18.5 Returns Reports

Purpose: Lists purchase returns and sale returns with quantity, price, total and reason.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Sales & Inventory → Returns tab

Procedure:

  1. Generate the report for your dates.
  2. Press the Returns tab and read the two tables.

How to verify: Each return appears with its original bill or sale number.

18.6 Parties Reports (supplier and customer balances)

Purpose: Lists every supplier (opening, bills, paid, balance, status) and customer (opening, sales, received, balance, status).

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Sales & Inventory → Parties tab

Procedure:

  1. Press the Parties tab.
  2. Read the supplier and customer tables; use CSV and Excel on each.

How to verify: Pick one party and compare its Balance with its ledger.

Important noteThe Customers page 'Sales' column is net of sale returns; check the column heading in each table before comparing numbers.

18.7 Profit & Loss Statement

Purpose: Shows income, operating expenses and the resulting profit for a period.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Profit & Loss

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Profit & Loss.
  2. Choose From and To and press Apply.
  3. Read Income and Operating Expenses; use Print.

How to verify: An expense you posted in the range appears under Operating Expenses.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
The statement shows nothing.The range defaults to 1 January until today; widen it or check the document dates and that nothing was reversed.

18.8 Balance Sheet

Purpose: Shows what you own (Assets), owe (Liabilities) and the owners' interest (Equity) on a given day.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Balance Sheet

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Balance Sheet.
  2. Choose Balance As Of and press Show.
  3. Check the badge, read Assets, Liabilities and Equity; Trial Balance opens that report.

How to verify: The badge reads 'Balanced — Assets = Liabilities + Equity'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Out of balance by N.Open the Reconciliation report, download a backup and report it; this should not happen.

18.9 Trial Balance

Purpose: Lists every account's debit and credit balance on a date.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Trial Balance

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Trial Balance.
  2. Choose Balance As Of, press Show.
  3. Read Code, Account, Type, Debit, Credit.

How to verify: The badge reads 'Balanced — Debits = Credits'.

18.10 Cash Flow Statement

Purpose: Shows where cash actually came from and went, so you can see the difference between profit and cash.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Cash Flow Statement

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Cash Flow Statement.
  2. Choose From and To, press Apply.
  3. Read the sections and the Reconciliation box.

How to verify: The badge reads 'Reconciled — opening + movement = closing'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Does not reconcile — a journal entry may be incomplete.Stop and report it with a screenshot.

18.11 AP/AR Aging

Purpose: Shows who owes you and whom you owe, grouped by how old the amount is: 0–30, 31–60, 61–90 and 90+ days.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → AP/AR Aging

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → AP/AR Aging.
  2. Read Accounts Payable Aging (Suppliers) and Accounts Receivable Aging (Customers); use Print.

How to verify: Compare one party's Total Due with its ledger balance.

18.12 Cash Book

Purpose: Lists every movement through cash and bank with cash in and cash out.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Cash Book

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Cash Book.
  2. Choose From, To and optionally Method, press Apply.

How to verify: Date, Type, Description, Method, Cash In and Cash Out are listed for the range.

18.13 Tax Summary

Purpose: Lists tax on purchases (Input Tax) and tax on sales (Output Tax) for a period.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Tax Summary

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Tax Summary.
  2. Choose From and To, press Apply.
  3. Read Input Tax — Purchases and Output Tax — Sales.

How to verify: Each listed document shows its tax amount.

18.14 Subledger Reconciliation

Purpose: Compares each control account in the general ledger with the subledger underneath it.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: Reports → Reconciliation

Procedure:

  1. Open Reports → Reconciliation.
  2. Read Control Account, Subledger, General Ledger, Difference and Status.
  3. Read the lists of customers and suppliers that make up Accounts Receivable and Accounts Payable.

How to verify: Every row has a Difference of zero and a good Status.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
A row shows a Difference.Post nothing more; download a backup and report it with a screenshot. A manual ledger adjustment on a customer or supplier can be a cause, because it is not posted to the general ledger.

19. Operating practices

Routines, corrections and troubleshooting. These boxes describe how to combine the facilities above.

19.1 First-time setup order

Purpose: The order in which to prepare a new installation.

Who can use it: An admin.

Procedure:

  1. Create your users (Admin → Users) and set roles.
  2. Check Accounting → Chart of Accounts and Fiscal Years & Periods.
  3. Add your cash and bank accounts (Admin → Financial Accounts) with their opening balances.
  4. Enable your business categories (Admin → Business Configuration); turn on batch tracking and expiry if you sell medicines or dated goods.
  5. Add warehouses if you have more than one (Admin → Warehouses) and, if needed, restrict users (Admin → Location Access).
  6. Add items (or Bulk Import) with opening stock, then suppliers and customers with opening balances.
  7. Open the Balance Sheet; it must say Balanced.
  8. Only then record purchases, sales and payments.

How to verify: The Balance Sheet is Balanced and Stock Valuation shows your opening stock.

Important noteOpening balances are real accounting entries; enter them before trading.

19.2 Month-end routine

Purpose: A checklist to close a month with confidence.

Who can use it: A manager or admin; closing a period is admin only.

Procedure:

  1. Record every invoice, payment, receipt and expense for the month, and post every draft.
  2. Run Accounting → Fixed Assets → Run Monthly Depreciation for the month.
  3. Open Reports → Reconciliation; every Difference must be zero. If not, stop and find out why.
  4. Open Reports → Balance Sheet; it must say Balanced; read Profit & Loss.
  5. Download a backup (Admin → Backup & Restore).
  6. If wanted, close the period (Fiscal Years & Periods → Close) so its figures cannot change.

How to verify: Reconciliation and Balance Sheet are green and a fresh backup file exists.

19.3 Year-end routine

Purpose: What to do after the last month of a year.

Who can use it: An admin.

Procedure:

  1. Complete the month-end routine for the last month.
  2. Open Fiscal Years & Periods and press Close Year.
  3. Create the next fiscal year if it does not exist (Create fiscal year).

How to verify: The Balance Sheet carries forward unchanged and the new year's Profit & Loss starts at zero.

Important noteInvoice numbers restart for the new year.

19.4 Fixing a mistake: edit, correct, reverse or return?

Purpose: Helps you choose the right way to put a wrong document right.

Who can use it: Everyone reads; edit is for managers, correct and reverse for admins.

Procedure:

  1. Draft purchase or sale: edit it (manager) or delete it (admin). Nothing was booked.
  2. Posted purchase or sale with a small error: an admin uses Correct with a reason.
  3. Posted document that should never have existed, or any payment, receipt, expense, return or stock adjustment: an admin presses Reverse, then you enter it again correctly.
  4. Goods physically went back to the supplier or came back from the customer: record a Purchase Return or Sale Return, not a reversal.

How to verify: The wrong document shows reversed or corrected and the right one is listed.

Important noteA reversal can be refused when undoing it would make stock negative, for example reversing a purchase whose goods have been sold.

19.5 Troubleshooting: a figure looks wrong

Purpose: Where to look first when a report disagrees with what you expect.

Who can use it: Everyone.

Procedure:

  1. Check the date range of the report; a document dated outside it is not included.
  2. Check the document's Status: a draft is not counted; a reversed one is excluded.
  3. Check the warehouse filter on stock pages.
  4. For stock value remember the cost is a weighted average, not the last price paid.
  5. Open Reports → Reconciliation; if a Difference appears, stop posting and report it.

How to verify: After fixing the filter or posting the draft, the figure agrees.

19.6 Troubleshooting: the system refuses an action

Purpose: What the common refusal messages mean.

Who can use it: Everyone.

Procedure:

  1. Read the message; it names the reason.
  2. '... is posted to the general ledger and can no longer be changed. Reverse it instead.' — use Reverse (admin) or Correct (admin).
  3. 'Insufficient stock' — the chosen warehouse holds less than the document needs.
  4. '... is closed. Post to an open period.' — use a date in an open month or ask an admin to reopen it.
  5. 'Access denied' or a greyed-out menu item — your role cannot do it; ask a manager or admin.

How to verify: After the fix the same action is accepted.

19.7 Safe practices: backups and cautions

Purpose: Habits that protect your data.

Who can use it: An admin.

Procedure:

  1. Download a backup every week (Admin → Backup & Restore), and before a restore, a reset or a year close.
  2. Keep backups on another computer or drive.
  3. Never share the Developer Tasks password or an admin account.
  4. Do not use Delete Transactions or Delete Everything on a live system unless you intend to start again.

How to verify: A dated backup file exists outside the main computer.

20. HR: employees, departments and designations

Menu HR & Payroll (optional module). These menus appear only when the technical staff have switched the module on (Developer Tasks → Optional Modules). All HR modules are off by default.

20.1 View and Filter Employees

Purpose: Lists employees with code, name, department, designation, joining date, status, basic salary and active flag.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Employees

Procedure:

  1. Open HR & Payroll → Employees.
  2. Type in Search (name, code, phone or email) and/or choose Department, Designation, Status or Active, then press Filter.
  3. Use Print PDF, or the buttons New Employee, Departments and Designations.

How to verify: Employees are listed in code order.

20.2 Add an Employee

Purpose: Creates an employee record. The employee code is issued by the system.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Employees → New Employee

Procedure:

  1. Press New Employee.
  2. Enter Name and Joining Date (required); optionally CNIC, Department, Designation, Phone, Email, Address.
  3. Choose Employment Status: Permanent, Probation, Contract or Intern.
  4. Enter Basic Salary, Allowances and Deductions if you wish; keep Active employee ticked.
  5. Optionally record Documents held and Notes.
  6. Press the save button.

How to verify: The message reads 'Employee <code> added.'

Important notePayroll does not read these salary boxes: it uses the employee's Salary Structure (see Payroll).

20.3 View an Employee Profile

Purpose: Shows one employee's details and salary figures.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Employees → click an employee

Procedure:

  1. Open Employees and click the employee.
  2. Read Department, Designation, Joining Date, Status, Phone, Email, CNIC, Address, Basic Salary, Allowances, Deductions, Gross and Net.
  3. Press Edit to change, or Back.

How to verify: The details match the employee form.

20.4 Edit an Employee

Purpose: Changes an employee's details. The employee code never changes.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: An employee's profile → Edit

Procedure:

  1. Press Edit on the profile.
  2. Change the fields.
  3. Press the save button (or Cancel).

How to verify: The message reads 'Employee <code> updated.'

20.5 Delete an Employee

Purpose: Removes an employee who has no history.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Employees → delete button

Procedure:

  1. Press the delete button on the row and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Employee <code> deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... has ... history, so the record was deactivated instead of deleted.An employee with attendance, leave or payroll history is made inactive, not removed.

20.6 Link an Employee to a Login

Purpose: Lets an employee sign in and use the My Work menu for their own attendance, leave and payslips.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: An employee's profile → Link a login

Procedure:

  1. Open the employee's profile.
  2. In Link a login choose the user account (or '— none (unlink) —' to remove the link).
  3. Press Save.

How to verify: The message reads '<email> is now linked to <employee>.' and that person sees My Work after signing in.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... is already linked to ...One login can belong to only one employee.
That user was not found.Choose from the list.

20.7 Departments: view and add

Purpose: Keeps the list of departments used on employee records.

Who can use it: Any verified user views; managers and admins add.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Departments

Procedure:

  1. Open HR & Payroll → Departments.
  2. Type the Name (required) and optional Description.
  3. Press Add.

How to verify: The message reads 'Department '<name>' added.' and it appears with its employee count.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Department name is required.Type a name.
Department '...' already exists.Use the existing one.

20.8 Delete a Department

Purpose: Removes an unused department.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Departments → delete button

Procedure:

  1. Press the delete button on the row and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Department '<name>' deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
'...' still has employees, so it cannot be deleted.Move the employees to another department first.

20.9 Designations: view and add

Purpose: Keeps the list of job titles used on employee records.

Who can use it: Any verified user views; managers and admins add.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Designations

Procedure:

  1. Open HR & Payroll → Designations.
  2. Type the Name and optional Description.
  3. Press Add.

How to verify: The message reads 'Designation '<name>' added.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Designation name is required.Type a name.
Designation '...' already exists.Use the existing one.

20.10 Delete a Designation

Purpose: Removes an unused job title.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Designations → delete button

Procedure:

  1. Press the delete button on the row and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Designation '<name>' deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
'...' still has employees, so it cannot be deleted.Reassign those employees first.

21. Attendance

Menu HR & Payroll (optional Attendance module; needs HR). These menus appear only when the technical staff have switched the module on (Developer Tasks → Optional Modules). All HR modules are off by default.

21.1 Mark Daily Attendance

Purpose: Marks every employee for one day on a single page.

Who can use it: Any verified user views; managers and admins save.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Daily Attendance

Procedure:

  1. Open Daily Attendance; use Previous, Today, Next or the Date box and Go to pick the day.
  2. For each employee choose a status (Present, Absent, Late, Half Day or Leave), optionally Check-in, Check-out and Remarks.
  3. Press Save Attendance.

How to verify: The message reads 'Attendance for <day> saved — N new, M updated'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Attendance cannot be recorded for a future date.Choose today or an earlier day.

21.2 Attendance History

Purpose: Lists attendance records with filters.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Attendance History

Procedure:

  1. Open Attendance History.
  2. Filter by Employee, Department, Status, From and To; press Filter.
  3. Read Date, Code, Employee, Status, In, Out, Hours, OT and Remarks; use Print PDF.

How to verify: Records appear newest first by date.

21.3 Add a Single Attendance Record

Purpose: Records one employee's attendance for one day outside the daily page.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Attendance History → New Record

Procedure:

  1. Press New Record.
  2. Choose the Employee and Date, the Status, optional Check-in, Check-out and Remarks.
  3. Press the save button.

How to verify: The message reads 'Attendance recorded for <employee> on <date>.'

21.4 Edit an Attendance Record

Purpose: Corrects a record.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Attendance History → edit button on a row

Procedure:

  1. Press the edit button.
  2. Change the fields.
  3. Press the save button.

How to verify: The message reads 'Attendance updated.'

21.5 Delete an Attendance Record

Purpose: Removes a wrong record.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Attendance History → delete button

Procedure:

  1. Press the delete button on the row and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Attendance record deleted.'

21.6 Monthly Attendance Summary

Purpose: Counts present, late, half-day, absent and leave days, worked days, hours and overtime per employee for a month.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Monthly Summary

Procedure:

  1. Open Monthly Summary.
  2. Choose Month, Year and optionally Department; press Show.
  3. Use Print PDF.

How to verify: Each employee row shows Worked Days, Hours and Overtime.

22. Leave

Menu HR & Payroll (optional Leave module; needs HR). These menus appear only when the technical staff have switched the module on (Developer Tasks → Optional Modules). All HR modules are off by default.

22.1 Leave Balances

Purpose: Shows each employee's allocated, used and remaining days per leave type, with pending, approved and rejected counts.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Balances

Procedure:

  1. Open Leave Balances.
  2. Choose Employee and Year, press Show.
  3. Use Print PDF; buttons lead to New Request, Requests, Allocations and Types.

How to verify: Remaining equals Allocated minus Used.

22.2 Leave Types: view and add

Purpose: Defines kinds of leave such as casual or medical, and whether they are paid or drawn from an allocation.

Who can use it: Any verified user views; admins add.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Types

Procedure:

  1. Open Leave Types.
  2. Enter Code (an example: HAJJ) and Name.
  3. Tick Paid leave, Drawn from an allocation, Carry forward allowed as needed; fill Max days per year, Carry-forward limit and Description if wanted.
  4. Press Add Type.

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave type '<name>' added.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
You do not have permission to configure leave types.Only admins can.

22.3 Activate or Deactivate a Leave Type

Purpose: Stops a type being offered without deleting it.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Types → toggle button on the row

Procedure:

  1. Press the toggle button on the type.

How to verify: The message reads ''<name>' is now active.' or 'inactive.'

22.4 Delete a Leave Type

Purpose: Removes a custom leave type that was never used.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Types → delete button

Procedure:

  1. Press the delete button on the row and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave type '<name>' deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
'...' is a standard type and cannot be deleted. Deactivate it instead.Use the toggle.
'...' has leave history, so it was deactivated rather than deleted.Nothing more to do.

22.5 Allocate Leave Days to an Employee

Purpose: Gives an employee a number of days of a leave type for a year.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Allocations

Procedure:

  1. Open Leave Allocations.
  2. Choose Employee, Leave Type, Year; enter Allocated Days (an example: 12) and optional Notes.
  3. Press Save Allocation.

How to verify: The message reads 'Allocation saved for <employee>.' and the row shows Allocated, Used and Remaining.

22.6 Delete a Leave Allocation

Purpose: Removes an allocation made by mistake.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Allocations → delete button

Procedure:

  1. Press the delete button on the row and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Allocation deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
That allocation has leave taken against it and cannot be deleted.Cancel the leave first, or keep the allocation.

22.7 View Leave Requests

Purpose: Lists requests with employee, type, dates, days, status and reason.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Requests

Procedure:

  1. Open Leave Requests.
  2. Filter by Employee, Leave Type, Status (Draft, Pending, Approved, Rejected, Cancelled); press Filter.

How to verify: Each row shows its status and the actions allowed for you.

22.8 Create a Leave Request

Purpose: Raises a request for leave for an employee.

Who can use it: Any verified user may raise a request; approval is by managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Requests → New Request

Procedure:

  1. Press New Request.
  2. Choose Employee and Leave Type, Duration (full or half day), Start Date and End Date; optionally a Reason.
  3. Press Submit for Approval, or Save as Draft to finish later.

How to verify: The message says the request was submitted or saved as a draft; managers and admins get a notification when it is submitted.

22.9 Edit a Leave Request

Purpose: Changes a draft request.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Requests → edit button

Procedure:

  1. Press the edit button.
  2. Change the fields.
  3. Press Save Changes (or Submit for Approval).

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave request updated.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
A <status> request can no longer be edited.Only drafts can be edited.

22.10 Submit a Draft Leave Request

Purpose: Sends a saved draft for approval.

Who can use it: Any verified user.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Requests → submit button on a draft

Procedure:

  1. Press the submit button on the draft.

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave request submitted for approval.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Only a draft can be submitted.This request is already past the draft stage.

22.11 Approve a Leave Request

Purpose: Grants the leave; the days are taken from the employee's balance and the employee is notified.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Requests → approve button on a pending request

Procedure:

  1. Open Leave Requests and filter by Pending.
  2. Press the approve button.

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave approved for <employee> (N day(s)).'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... has N day(s) of <type> leftThe balance is too small; allocate more days or reject.
Payroll for <period> is already finalized and covers these dates.Cancel that payroll period first, or apply the leave to a later period.
Only a pending request can be approved.The request has already been decided.

22.12 Reject a Leave Request

Purpose: Refuses a pending request; the employee is notified.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Requests → reject button

Procedure:

  1. Press the reject button on a pending request.

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave request rejected.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Only a pending request can be rejected.It was already decided.

22.13 Cancel a Leave Request

Purpose: Cancels a request, including an approved one, and returns the days to the balance.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Leave Requests → cancel button

Procedure:

  1. Press the cancel button on the request and confirm.

How to verify: The message says the leave was cancelled and the days are back in the employee's balance.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
That request is already cancelled.Nothing to do.
Payroll for <period> is finalized and covers these dates.Cancel that payroll period first.

23. Payroll

Menu HR & Payroll (optional Payroll module; needs HR). Managers and admins view and prepare payroll; only admins finalize, pay, cancel or reverse payments, because those write to the general ledger. These menus appear only when the technical staff have switched the module on (Developer Tasks → Optional Modules). All HR modules are off by default.

23.1 Salary Structures: view

Purpose: Lists each employee's salary structure.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Salary Structures

Procedure:

  1. Open Salary Structures.
  2. Read Code, Employee, Effective From, Basic and Components.
  3. Press Edit on a row.

How to verify: Every employee you want paid shows a structure.

23.2 Set an Employee's Salary Structure

Purpose: Sets the amounts for Basic Salary, allowances, bonus and fixed deductions that payroll uses.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Salary Structures → Edit

Procedure:

  1. Press Edit on the employee.
  2. Enter an amount against each component (Basic Salary, House Rent Allowance, Medical Allowance, Conveyance Allowance, Other Allowance, Bonus, Tax, Loan Deduction, Other Deduction).
  3. Optionally add Notes.
  4. Press Save Structure.

How to verify: The message reads 'Salary structure saved for <employee>.'

Important noteOvertime, advance recovery and absence deductions are worked out automatically from attendance, leave and advances.

23.3 Create a Payroll Period

Purpose: Opens a pay period, for example one month.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Payroll → New Period

Procedure:

  1. Open Payroll and press New Period.
  2. Enter Period Name (an example: January 2026), Start Date and End Date; optional Notes.
  3. Press Create Period.

How to verify: The message reads 'Payroll period '<name>' created.' and it appears on the Payroll page as Draft.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Those exact dates already exist as a payroll period.Use the existing period.

23.4 Process Payroll for a Period

Purpose: Calculates a payslip for every employee who has a salary structure. It can be repeated until you are happy.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Payroll → Open a period → Process Payroll (Recalculate)

Procedure:

  1. Open the period.
  2. Press Process Payroll (it reads Recalculate once processed).
  3. Read each employee's Days, OT (h), Gross, Deductions and Net.

How to verify: The message reads '<period> processed — N payslip(s) calculated'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Skipped <employee>: ... no active salary structureGive that employee a Salary Structure and process again.
Important noteAttendance is used only when the Attendance module is on; with it off every employee is paid a full period.

23.5 Preview One Employee's Payroll

Purpose: Shows how one employee's pay would be calculated for a period.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: A payroll period's page → the employee row

Procedure:

  1. Open a period and use the employee's preview link.
  2. Read the calculation; press Back.

How to verify: The preview matches the payslip after processing.

23.6 Finalize a Payroll Period

Purpose: Locks the payslips and posts one journal entry for the period: salary expense debited, salaries payable, tax payable and advances credited.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: A payroll period's page → Finalize

Procedure:

  1. Make sure the period is processed and checked.
  2. Press Finalize and confirm.

How to verify: The message says the period was finalized; View Journal Entry appears.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Nothing to finalize — process the period first.Press Process Payroll first.
... was not finalized — accounting refused the entry: ...Read the reason (for example a closed period).
Important noteAdmins receive a notification when a period is finalized.

23.7 Pay Salaries

Purpose: Records the salary actually paid from a cash or bank account.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: A finalized payroll period's page → Pay Salary

Procedure:

  1. Open a finalized period.
  2. Enter Amount, Payment Date and optional Reference, and choose the account.
  3. Press Pay Salary.

How to verify: The message says a salary payment was recorded for the period; the payment appears in the period's payments table.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
Pick the cash or bank account the salary was paid from.Choose an account.
Payment amount must be more than zero.Enter an amount.
<period> is already fully paid.Nothing to pay.
only a finalized ...Finalize the period first.

23.8 Reverse a Salary Payment

Purpose: Cancels a recorded salary payment.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: A period's payments table → reverse button

Procedure:

  1. Press the reverse button on the payment and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Salary payment reversed'.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
That payment has already been reversed.Nothing to do.

23.9 Cancel a Payroll Period

Purpose: Undoes a finalized period by reversing its journal entry.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: A period's page → Cancel Period

Procedure:

  1. Reverse any salary payments first.
  2. Press Cancel Period and confirm.

How to verify: The message says the period was cancelled; admins are notified.

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... cannot be cancelled because salary payments exist.Reverse the payments first.

23.10 Delete a Payroll Period

Purpose: Removes a period that was never finalized.

Who can use it: Admin only.

Where to find it: A period's page → Delete

Procedure:

  1. Press Delete and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Payroll period '<name>' deleted.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
... is finalized and cannot be deleted. Cancel it instead.Use Cancel Period.

23.11 View and Print a Payslip

Purpose: Shows one employee's payslip for a period.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: A period's page → Payslip link on the employee row

Procedure:

  1. Open the period and press the payslip link.
  2. Press Print, or Back.

How to verify: The Net Salary shown equals the Net on the period page.

23.12 Record an Employee Advance

Purpose: Records money lent to an employee that payroll will recover in instalments.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Employee Advances

Procedure:

  1. Open Employee Advances.
  2. Choose Employee, Date, Amount, optional Instalment per period and Remarks.
  3. Press Record.

How to verify: The message reads 'Advance recorded for <employee>.' and the row shows Amount, Recovered and Outstanding.

23.13 Cancel an Employee Advance

Purpose: Cancels an advance entered by mistake.

Who can use it: Managers and admins.

Where to find it: HR & Payroll → Employee Advances → cancel button

Procedure:

  1. Press the cancel button on the row and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Advance cancelled.'

24. My Work (employee self-service)

The My Work menu appears only for a user whose login is linked to an employee record, and only for the modules that are on.

24.1 My Work: My Profile

Purpose: Shows your own employee details.

Who can use it: A user linked to an employee.

Where to find it: My Work → My Profile

Procedure:

  1. Open My Work → My Profile.
  2. Use the buttons My Attendance, My Leaves, My Payslips.

How to verify: Department, Designation, Joining Date, Status and contact details are shown.

24.2 My Work: My Attendance

Purpose: Shows your attendance for a month.

Who can use it: A user linked to an employee.

Where to find it: My Work → My Attendance

Procedure:

  1. Open My Attendance.
  2. Choose Month and Year, press Show.

How to verify: Date, Status, In, Out, Hours, Overtime and Remarks are listed.

24.3 My Work: My Leaves

Purpose: Shows your leave balances and your requests.

Who can use it: A user linked to an employee.

Where to find it: My Work → My Leaves

Procedure:

  1. Open My Leaves.
  2. Choose the year.
  3. Read Allocated, Used, Remaining and your requests with their status.

How to verify: Remaining equals Allocated minus Used.

24.4 My Work: Request Leave

Purpose: Lets you ask for leave for yourself.

Who can use it: A user linked to an employee.

Where to find it: My Work → My Leaves → Request Leave

Procedure:

  1. Press Request Leave.
  2. Choose Leave Type, Duration, Start Date, End Date and optional Reason.
  3. Press Submit Request.

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave request submitted for approval (N day(s)).'

Important noteA manager or admin decides the request; you are notified of the decision.

24.5 My Work: Withdraw a Leave Request

Purpose: Withdraws a request you made, while it can still be withdrawn.

Who can use it: A user linked to an employee.

Where to find it: My Work → My Leaves → withdraw button on the request

Procedure:

  1. Press the withdraw button on the request and confirm.

How to verify: The message reads 'Leave request withdrawn.'

Common mistakes:

What you seeWhat to do
This request is <status> and can no longer be ...Only an early-stage request can be withdrawn; ask a manager.

24.6 My Work: My Payslips

Purpose: Lists your payslips by period with gross, deductions and net.

Who can use it: A user linked to an employee.

Where to find it: My Work → My Payslips

Procedure:

  1. Open My Payslips.
  2. Press View on a period.

How to verify: Each row shows Period, From, To, Gross, Deductions and Net.

24.7 My Work: View and Print My Payslip

Purpose: Shows one of your payslips for printing.

Who can use it: A user linked to an employee.

Where to find it: My Work → My Payslips → View

Procedure:

  1. Press View on a payslip.
  2. Press Print, or Back.

How to verify: The Net Salary is shown at the top.
